# Changelog

All notable changes to this project will be documented in this file.

## [Unreleased]

### 🚀 Features

- **The orchestrator now remembers who caused it to speak to an agent** ([ISSUE-0124](docs/issues/ISSUE-0124-orchestrator-hop-drops-tenant-on-agent-cascade.md), the [ISSUE-0082](docs/issues/ISSUE-0082-orchestrator-per-request-session-principal-emission.md) **R-2** residual, v0.3.15 PR A1 — landed **dormant**): v0.3.14 partitioned a persona's memory by which authenticated person is speaking, and that boundary holds for every turn's own write — but a persona's **reply** re-enters the orchestrator as a fresh *unauthenticated* REST publish, so the whole cascade below it dispatches with no tenant at all. Measured live on 2026-08-07: **9 of 15 dispatches** in a single interaction descending from one authenticated publish carried no principal, including one persona relaying Alice's context to two others with the tenant stripped. Those relayed turns land in the shared `'local'` tenant — the one every agent-origin and autonomous turn resolves — and [RFC 0049](docs/rfcs/0049-memory-consolidation-gradient.md) Phase 1 facts are cross-room by default, so a restatement of A's disclosure becomes speakable into rooms A is not in. **The obvious fix is unavailable and its near-miss is worse**: having the persona echo the principal back would make the orchestrator trust an agent-supplied identity claim, and the persona binds `principal_scope` from that value with strict-equality recall — so an unauthenticated caller could name any tenant and *read* it, trading a write leak for a cross-tenant **read** primitive. The same objection kills echoing the stimulus **message id**, since an agent sees other members' ids in channel history and a chosen id resolves to a chosen principal. **What lands here is the shape that is left**: state the orchestrator already knows, held server-side, never accepted from the wire. `GRPCMessageDispatcher.Dispatch` is the one place that knows both halves of a true statement — which agent it handed a stimulus to, and which principal that stimulus descended from — so it now records `(channel, agent) → principal` in an in-memory table. **Every degradation fails closed**, because an entry holds the stimuli that are still *live* and derives its answer from them rather than storing one: a pair resolves only when exactly one stimulus is outstanding and that stimulus has a principal. Two live principals resolve nothing (a last-write-wins table would attribute the agent's reply to whoever spoke most recently), and neither does one principal racing an **unauthenticated** stimulus — an agent-origin turn, an autonomous tick, a convene — which is equally able to be the turn the agent is answering and has no principal to name. Deriving this rather than latching a flag is what lets a busy room **recover**: a second speaker who says one thing stops mattering one turn budget later, where a stored flag refreshed by every later dispatch would instead have pinned an active room to `'local'` for as long as the conversation lasted — and a cascade keeps itself busy by construction. An entry past its 120s turn budget is simply gone. **Expiry alone is not enough to recover, though**, and the second mechanism is what keeps the table useful in exactly the rooms the defect lives in: ageing out only helps when the competing stimulus stops being restated, which is true of a second human speaker but *not* of the orchestrator's own principal-less forced turns — the RFC 0052 convener cadence, a synthesis-close timeout, a chair escalation descending from either — which recur faster than the turn budget and so keep an anonymous stimulus permanently live, leaving the pair forever ambiguous and its row never reclaimable. So the read that re-stamps a reply also **retires the stimuli that reply answered**: an agent that publishes has answered whatever it was holding, which is the only evidence the orchestrator ever gets that a stimulus is *spent* rather than merely young. What stays irreducible is stated rather than papered over — a stimulus the agent has not yet answered is genuinely a candidate for its next reply, so an authenticated stimulus racing an unanswered anonymous one is ambiguous and must remain so; only a correlation key from the agent could rank them, and any key the agent supplies is disqualified. All of these leave the publish at `'local'` — today's behaviour — so a *missed* attribution is the only failure mode, never a wrong one. **The record follows the stimulus the orchestrator actually asked a turn for.** It is written after the receiver's ack, so a dispatch that was dropped, refused or never dialled leaves nothing behind — and only for a recipient the router *elected* to answer, because that ack is **pre-ingest** (the servicer returns as soon as the wake is accepted; the response gate runs later) and the fanout deliberately delivers to members that gate will silence, so they ingest the room rather than going amnesiac. Those hold the stimulus and never answer it, so an entry for one would be inherited by that agent's next *unrelated* publish. The election is [`orderResponders`](internal/channels/floor_control.go) — the orchestrator's own superset of the receiver gate's respond-true set, already trusted for the RFC 0048 presence signal — so the write reuses it rather than re-deriving the agent's gate in Go; the residual it leaves is stated in the code: an elected member whose RFC 0030 salience bid lands below threshold is an LLM-side judgement the orchestrator cannot predict, and the TTL is its only bound. **In memory only, and single-orchestrator** — a *stale* attribution is a mis-attribution, so losing the table on restart (everything falls back to `'local'`) is the safer failure, and a reply routed to a different orchestrator than its stimulus degrades rather than guessing. **No behaviour change in this PR**: nothing reads the table until PR 2 lands the re-stamp in `ChannelRouter.Publish`, and a dispatch made with a *populated* table is pinned to send byte-identical metadata and payload to one made with no table at all. **An unauthenticated dispatch is recorded too**, under an anonymous key that can only ever make a pair ambiguous and never resolve one: skipping it would have let the *next* authenticated stimulus — arriving while the anonymous one was still live — create a clean single-stimulus row and resolve a principal the agent may never have been answering, which is precisely the wrong answer the design forbids. So under `auth.mode: disabled` the table holds one anonymous-only row per dispatched-to `(channel, agent)` pair rather than staying empty: nothing it holds can resolve, the size stays inside the same `channels × members` bound, and the sweep reclaims it one turn budget after the traffic stops. Gates: `internal/channels/principal_attribution_test.go`, `internal/channels/grpc_dispatcher_attribution_test.go`, `internal/channels/dispatch_expects_reply_test.go`.

- **A persona's reply now keeps the tenant of the person who caused it** ([ISSUE-0124](docs/issues/ISSUE-0124-orchestrator-hop-drops-tenant-on-agent-cascade.md), the [ISSUE-0082](docs/issues/ISSUE-0082-orchestrator-per-request-session-principal-emission.md) **R-2** residual, v0.3.15 PR A2 — the store PR A1 landed dormant now has its reader): when an authenticated person publishes into a room, the orchestrator dispatches every turn under their principal — but a persona's **reply** re-enters as a fresh *unauthenticated* REST publish, and until now the entire cascade below it dispatched with no tenant at all. Measured live on 2026-08-07, that was **9 of 15 dispatches** in a single interaction, including one persona relaying Alice's context to two others with the tenant stripped; those relayed turns landed in the shared `'local'` tenant, which every agent-origin and autonomous turn resolves, so a restatement of A's disclosure became speakable into rooms A is not in. The publish path now asks the [PR A1 attribution table](docs/issues/ISSUE-0124-orchestrator-hop-drops-tenant-on-agent-cascade.md) who caused this agent to speak and, on a live unambiguous hit, puts that principal back on the context — so the reply and everything descending from it stay in the causing person's tenant, transitively, bounded by `cascade_depth`. **Nothing is accepted from the agent**: the orchestrator answers from state it recorded itself at the dispatch chokepoint, which is why the fix cannot be the persona echoing a principal back (the persona binds `principal_scope` from that value with strict-equality recall, so an agent-supplied claim would be a cross-tenant **read** primitive). **Every degradation is fail-closed and lands exactly where it does today** — an ambiguous room (two people with live stimuli to one agent), an expired stimulus (past the 120s turn budget), an autonomous or tick-origin publish, and every publish under `auth.mode: disabled` all keep resolving `'local'`. The read is *consuming*: an agent that publishes has answered what it was holding, which is the only evidence the orchestrator ever gets that a stimulus is spent rather than merely young — without it, a busy autonomous room stays permanently ambiguous. **Accepted and stated**: this grants an agent a bounded **write** into the tenant of someone it is already talking to — never a read, and never the ability to name a principal. **Known gap**: single-orchestrator, since the table is in memory; a reply routed to a different orchestrator than its stimulus degrades to `'local'` rather than guessing. Alone this closes R-2's wire half — the close-time aggregate still spans speakers until R-1 lands ([ISSUE-0123](docs/issues/ISSUE-0123-per-speaker-interaction-scope.md)), so the boundary claim is not complete until both ship in this release.

### 🐛 Fixes

- **The fleet comes back on its own after an orchestrator restart** ([ISSUE-0125](docs/issues/ISSUE-0125-agents-never-reregister-after-orchestrator-restart.md), v0.3.15 PR C1): the orchestrator's agent registry is in-memory and every agent registered exactly once, in its **own** startup path — so when the *orchestrator* was the process that restarted, the registry came back empty and nothing ever told it who was out there again. The deployment looked healthy the whole time (`/healthz` green, containers up, publishes answering `201`) while every dispatch was dropped and the personas simply never replied, until an operator restarted each agent by hand. Recorded as an operational quirk since the v0.3.0 execution report and as v0.3.2's F-6 at severity *low*; what changed is the blast radius, not the behaviour — the group-channel path makes the same condition **silent** rather than loud, and on 2026-08-07 it voided a full live MT arc on a paid provider before anyone noticed. **The fix is the signal the agent already holds**: each agent watches the connectivity state of its long-lived gRPC channel to the orchestrator (opened at startup, shared by the RFC 0018 log shipper and the RFC 0023 wallet client) and re-registers whenever that channel **leaves `READY` and returns**. A reconnect gets **several tries, not one**: the channel that wakes the agent is the orchestrator's gRPC port, but registration is a REST POST to a different port that may still be binding, and one attempt into that window would leave the agent unregistered until a next reconnect that may never come — so each reconnect retries with a short backoff and only a run of failures gives up, at ERROR. The watch loop is supervised for the same reason, so a stray channel error cannot quietly end re-registration for the life of the process. Deliberately *not* the `READY → TRANSIENT_FAILURE → READY` cycle the issue's own fix sketch named: `TRANSIENT_FAILURE` is only entered when a connection *attempt* fails, and on a clean restart the shipper's stream EOFs with no RPC pending, so the idle channel goes to `IDLE` — a watcher written to the literal cycle passes an injected unit test and never fires in production. Event-driven rather than a heartbeat, because a fleet-wide timer is the polling shape [RFC 0024](docs/rfcs/0024-event-driven-scheduling.md) removed. **Reconnecting re-registers and nothing else** — `_self_register()` and the catch-up replay are adjacent in the startup path, and catch-up has no watermark ([RFC 0011](docs/rfcs/0011-channels-bridges.md) OQ #8), so re-running the tail on every orchestrator blip would be unbounded re-derivation; the watcher takes one callback and calls it, pinned by a test that a reconnect fetches no history. **Two pieces ride with it.** `registry.Register` is now an **upsert** — the precondition every re-registration shape shares: it used to return `ErrAgentAlreadyRegistered`, surfaced as `409 CONFLICT`, so a re-register was a no-op against a *populated* registry and an agent that moved could never correct its advertised address (`POST /api/v1/agents/register` now answers `201` for both an insert and an update, "registration accepted"; [RFC 0001](docs/rfcs/0001-core-orchestration-pipeline.md) and [RFC 0002](docs/rfcs/0002-rest-api-server.md) amended). And an orchestrator holding **zero** registered agents while channels still have members is now logged at **ERROR**, once per outage and re-armed by any dispatch that resolves — recovery has to re-arm off the *success* path, because a deployment whose members are all registered again produces no miss to carry it, which would silently demote the signal to once per process — the per-recipient `dispatch target not registered` WARN stays at warn, because one absent member is the normal cost of a standing human participant or a mistyped membership, and the whole defect was that those two states looked identical. The upsert and the watcher are pinned by **separate** tests on purpose: a restarted orchestrator's registry is empty, so the upsert passes even when nothing ever re-registers. **Manual-test cleanup** ([ISSUE-0126](docs/issues/ISSUE-0126-mt-orchestrator-restart-registry-note-missing.md) option 1): both restart warnings are deleted — `MT-MEMORY-MULTIUSER-001`'s and `MT-MEMORY-GROUP-TENANT-001`'s — and the eight unguarded restart steps are safe as written, with no persona restart needed. Three things were kept because re-registration does not fix them: wait for `/healthz` before the next turn, `GET /api/v1/agents` as the check when a leg goes quiet, and F-6's second half — the RFC 0009 rate-limiter bucket is still not flushed by a restart, so the first turn after one can draw `429` for ~60 s. **Live verification is still outstanding**: both issues stay open until the v0.3.15 release gate exercises a real restart. Gates: `internal/registry/registry_upsert_test.go`, `internal/server/agent_register_upsert_test.go`, `internal/channels/grpc_dispatcher_empty_fleet_test.go`, `tests/unit/python/test_reregistration_watcher.py`.

## [0.3.14] - 2026-08-19

> **Codename:** One persona, many people

### ✨ Highlights

- **One persona, many people — a persona's memory is now partitioned by *which authenticated person is speaking*.** Under `auth.mode: enabled` two people sharing one channel get per-speaker persona memory: neither person's turn recalls the other's disclosures, including through the deliberately cross-room tiers. Room continuity is untouched — the transcript is not principal-scoped, so both still see the same conversation.
- **A rail armed since 2026-05-29 is finally fed.** [ISSUE-0081](docs/issues/ISSUE-0081-session-id-process-global-not-task-local.md) shipped the persona-side tenant vertical — `principal_id` on all five memory tiers, strict-equality recall, the `persatrix-principal` header — but nothing ever *emitted* a principal, so every caller resolved the one shared `local` tenant. Shipping auth made the caller's `participant_id` a **verified** claim; the orchestrator now stamps it once at the middleware that wraps the root mux, because a missed dispatch origin fails *open*.
- **The live arc found two defects and both were fixed before the tag, not deferred.** `persatrix chat` — the primary CLI verb — could not authenticate **at all** under `enabled`, on exactly the surface this release activates; and a restart re-derived one person's private content into the shared `local` tenant on every boot ([ISSUE-0130](docs/issues/ISSUE-0130-catchup-replay-rederives-memory-under-default-principal.md)).
- **A person's erasure request stops at their own memory.** `delete_by_subject` — the RFC 0013 GDPR traversal point — is scoped to the principal on both DELETEs *and their returned counts*, closing a privacy boundary that would otherwise have broken on the day the tenant boundary shipped.
- **The agent↔orchestrator channel payload has a written contract** ([RFC 0040](docs/rfcs/0040-agent-orchestrator-transport-unification.md) Phase 1) — written down *before* the Phase 2 dual-surface window opens, when drift would be hardest to spot.
- **No store migrations — but this is the one release where drop-in schema does not mean drop-in behaviour.** A deployment already running `auth.mode: enabled` finds each person's accumulated memory unreachable the day emission lands: a partition, not a deletion. `auth.mode: disabled` is byte-identical. See the Upgrade Notes.

### 🚀 Features

- **One persona, many people — the tenant boundary is live** ([ISSUE-0082](docs/issues/ISSUE-0082-orchestrator-per-request-session-principal-emission.md) Part 2, v0.3.14 PR 2): the orchestrator now **emits** a verified principal, so under `auth.mode: enabled` a persona's memory is partitioned by *which authenticated person is speaking* — not just by room (session) and run (epoch). The source is the RFC 0039 §F verified `participant_id`, the same value the chat surface already stamps as `user_id`, so the tenant dimension and the conversation surface name one identity and an account rename never re-partitions anyone's memory. Two people sharing one channel now get per-speaker persona memory; neither person's *turn* recalls the other's disclosures (room continuity is unaffected — transcript and verbatim history are not principal-scoped). The boundary is **per-turn** — see the two stated residuals below for the derived and relayed writes it does not yet cover. **The threading is structural, not per-handler**: the principal is stamped once in `authMiddleware`, which wraps the root mux, because a missed dispatch origin fails *open* — silently collapsing to the shared `'local'` tenant with no error and no red test. A route added later inherits it automatically; the origin enumeration (`dispatchOriginClassification`) survives as the reviewable statement of where the boundary lands, pinned by a route-table test that parses the package's own registrations and **fails on any unclassified route**. Also fixed on the same rail: `ChannelRouter.onSynthesisTimeout` ran the bounded-close teardown on a bare `context.Background()`, so an authenticated person's close-notification fan — the members' *final* turn, the one the RFC 0020 metered summary is built from — would have landed in `'local'` while that person's own published turns were partitioned; the arming request's principal is now stashed on the pending-synthesis record and re-stamped at fire time. (The synthesis-reply and end-vote closes descend from the chair persona's own unauthenticated publish and so still carry none — a deliberate asymmetry, for the reason in the aggregate-close residual below.) **`auth.mode: disabled` is byte-identical** — no header is emitted, every request resolves `'local'`, and deterministic tests pin the no-delta; the same holds for every unauthenticated caller, which is the entire persona fleet (agents hold no accounts and drive the §Non-Goals public REST seams), so agent-origin turns keep collapsing to `'local'` by design. **Known, accepted, and stated**: migration v11 backfilled every pre-existing row to `'local'` and the predicate is strict equality with no carve-out, so a deployment already running `enabled` finds its accumulated persona memory unreachable the day emission lands — a partition, not a deletion (the corpus stays reachable under `'local'`). Bridging it would *be* the cross-tenant bridge the boundary forbids. **Two residuals of the same shape — the boundary is per-turn, and a derived or relayed write does not inherit it.** *Aggregate close*: the persona's RFC 0020 interaction scope is the ROOM, not the speaker, so in a group channel every speaker's turns accumulate into one record whose close-time summary **and extracted facts** land under whichever principal the closing turn carried — and facts are cross-room by default, so in a shared room a fact derived from B's disclosure can be written under A's principal. The real fix is per-speaker interaction scope (an RFC 0020 §G change, **re-slotted v0.4.0 → v0.3.15** so interaction functionality is complete before v0.4.0 organizations build on it); consistently stamping the close paths either way would make the aggregate systematically single-tenant or put a person's own summaries out of their own reach, so the asymmetry is left visible instead. *Agent cascades*: a persona's reply re-enters as a fresh unauthenticated publish, so orchestrator-mediated cascade turns dispatch under `'local'` even inside an authenticated person's interaction (the in-process `EventDispatcher` cascade does forward the axis; only the REST hop loses it). Having the persona send the principal back is not the fix — trusting an agent-supplied claim would hand an unauthenticated caller a cross-tenant *read* primitive; the safe shape is server-side causal tracking, deferred. Neither residual is covered by `MT-MEMORY-MULTIUSER-001`, which drives a single-persona DM. Per-principal capacity sweeps (episode TTL, size-cap eviction, procedural decay, note prune) stay agent-global — capacity policy, not read-confidentiality; recall stays principal-filtered either way. Live proof ran at release-prep — the whole `MT-MEMORY-MULTIUSER-001` arc (authored here) passed with both emitted principal values recorded off storage; see Testing below.
- **The principal wire rail, landed dormant** ([ISSUE-0082](docs/issues/ISSUE-0082-orchestrator-per-request-session-principal-emission.md) Part 2, v0.3.14 PR 1): the orchestrator can now carry a per-request verified principal to the persona — `grpcmeta.MDPrincipal`/`InjectPrincipal` beside the session and epoch helpers, a `channels.WithPrincipal` request-ctx carrier that survives the fanout's `context.WithoutCancel` hop (the propagation property every dispatch descending from the detached request ctx rides on — a path that builds a *fresh* context carries no principal, and PR 2 owns closing the two known ones), the injection at the `GRPCMessageDispatcher.Dispatch` chokepoint with the `principal.id` span attribute the other two axes set, and the **wire-key lockstep guard** byte-matching `agents.principal_id.PRINCIPAL_METADATA_GRPC_KEY` (a rename on either side would silently disable tenant isolation while everything else stayed green). **No behaviour change**: nothing stamps a principal onto ctx yet — deterministic pins assert the header and span attribute are entirely absent until PR 2 lands the producer, which is also the future `auth.mode: disabled` no-delta contract.
- **The agent↔orchestrator channel payload has a written contract** ([RFC 0040](docs/rfcs/0040-agent-orchestrator-transport-unification.md) Phase 1, v0.3.14 PR 4): the channel publish and history payloads were an unwritten agreement between `agents/channel_publisher.py` and `internal/server/channel_handlers.go` — two hand-maintained shapes on either side of a REST hop, with nothing failing when they drifted. Phase 1 writes it down **before** the Phase 2 dual-surface window opens, when drift would be hardest to spot. Three definitions land in the existing `schemas/channel.schema.json` (`publishMessageRequest`, `channelMessage`, `channelHistoryResponse`), following the `messageMetadata` precedent already housed there rather than opening a new file; each carries `additionalProperties: false` as the trip-wire. **The RFC's own field list was stale** — it enumerated four publish fields, but the Go struct carries eight: `session_id` (RFC 0031 Phase 3) and `epoch_id` ([ISSUE-0085](docs/issues/ISSUE-0085-epoch-axis-run-isolation.md) PR 5) landed after the RFC was authored, plus `thread_id`/`channel_type`. All eight are pinned. **Send side**: `agents/channel_payload_contract.py` loads the schema once per process and compiles cached validators; `HTTPChannelPublisher` validates the assembled body at the last point before the wire, so the check sees exactly what the orchestrator will decode. **Fail-open by design** — a violation logs WARN and the publish proceeds, honouring RFC 0040's "no flag day" guarantee; the enforcement teeth are at test time, not in production traffic. **Decode side is a source-parity pin, not runtime validation**: `go.mod` carries no JSON-Schema library, and adding one to the publish hot path to power a fail-open WARN buys no signal the send-side validator and a drift pin do not already carry. `tests/unit/python/test_cross_language_channel_payload_drift.py` holds it instead, and **forks by marshalling direction** — the two structs Go *marshals* are pinned by struct-tag parity (`omitempty` ↔ `required`), while the request struct Go only *unmarshals* is pinned against `handlePublishMessage`'s own `== ""` 400s, because `encoding/json` ignores `omitempty` when decoding and the tags therefore say nothing about what a caller must send. Drift is caught when the edit is made rather than a release later. **Container note**: `Dockerfile.agent` now copies `schemas/` into site-packages, because the validator resolves the schema off `Path(__file__).parent.parent` — the same resolution the `prompts/` block documents. An `/app`-only copy would leave the validator a permanent no-op in every container, i.e. disabled in the one deployment where it observes live payloads. **No proto change and no transport change** — Phase 1 is schema plus tests over today's REST path, so it does not advance RFC 0040's `📋 Proposed` status; Phases 2–4 (the `OrchestratorService` proto and the agent-side migration) remain the v0.4.0 train. Gates: `tests/unit/python/test_channel_payload_contract.py` + the cross-language drift suite.

### 🐛 Fixes

- **A person's erasure request stops at their own memory** ([ISSUE-0081](docs/issues/ISSUE-0081-session-id-process-global-not-task-local.md) erasure residual, v0.3.14 PR 3): `FactStore.delete_by_subject` — the RFC 0013 / RFC 0026 §H GDPR traversal point for the `facts` tier — was scoped to `agent_id` only. That was harmless for exactly as long as every caller resolved the one shared `'local'` principal; PR 2 ended that, making this a **privacy boundary that would have broken on the same day it shipped**: one person's right-to-erasure call would have deleted another person's facts about the same subject. Both DELETEs now carry `AND principal_id = ?`, resolved through the same `resolve_active_principal` seam the recall and write paths use, so a caller erases exactly the rows it could read — the write-side mirror of the strict recall predicate. The **returned counts** are scoped with them, not just the deletes: that map is what RFC 0013's `SubjectErasure` audits as `records_deleted`, so a foreign row in the tally would disclose that another tenant holds facts about the subject even with the DELETE correctly scoped. The `session_id` and `epoch_id` axes are deliberately **not** scoped and are pinned that way — an erasure traversal must reach every row the principal ever wrote, including earlier rooms and earlier runs, and narrowing it to the caller's active room / run would be a *silent* GDPR miss rather than a visible failure. Single-tenant, `auth.mode: disabled`, and CLI deployments are a no-delta (every row resolves `'local'` on both paths). Gate: `tests/unit/python/test_facts_erasure_principal_scope.py`, in the ISSUE-0081 PR 4 carve-out idiom — *a foreign principal's erasure call can neither count nor delete another principal's rows*, on both traversal columns. **Known gap, filed not fixed**: the principal predicate has no `"*"` sentinel by design, so a subject cannot be erased across tenants — and an operator-initiated traversal has no principal of its own. The caller audit found no in-tree caller (the primitive still waits on RFC 0013's `SubjectErasure`, v0.5.0), so whether the right to be forgotten spans tenants is filed as [ISSUE-0127](docs/issues/ISSUE-0127-cross-principal-erasure-verb.md) for the RFC that owns the decision rather than answered by a predicate here.
- **A restart no longer copies one person's private content into the shared tenant** ([ISSUE-0130](docs/issues/ISSUE-0130-catchup-replay-rederives-memory-under-default-principal.md), v0.3.14 PR 1a — the R-3 residual found by the live tenant arc): on startup the RFC 0011 catch-up replays the last N channel messages, and those events carry **no principal** — the orchestrator's `messages` table has no principal column, so `_build_replay_event` had nothing to seed and the persona bound its default. The close path then summarised the replayed span and ran the RFC 0026 extractor over it, writing the result to `'local'` — where the whole persona fleet, every autonomous turn and every caller under `auth.mode: disabled` resolves. Catch-up has no watermark ([RFC 0011](docs/rfcs/0011-channels-bridges.md) OQ #8), so it re-ingested the window on **every** boot: unbounded, and observed growing `0 → 2 → 5` over the arc's first restarts. Note the person↔person boundary was never breached — `'local'` is not readable by another authenticated principal, which is why the arc's absence bar passed for the right reason; what leaked is **person→anonymous**. `Interaction` gains a `replayed` marker captured under the same frozen-at-open rule as `session_id`, and `persist_closed_interaction` skips derivation for a replayed span — no episode, so no extraction, so nothing lands in the shared tenant. **Reading the path narrowed the blast radius from the filed diagnosis**: replay events open tracker scopes but never close them, so a replayed turn appended to an already-open live interaction still closes under the live principal and derives normally; only a rotation close that opened *and* closed entirely while the agent was down was ever derived unattributed. **The marker alone over-suppressed**, though — that same never-closed scope meant the next live turn merged into the catch-up interaction and inherited the flag, eating the first conversation after every restart (the common mid-conversation case, and the only case for wire-untracked thread scopes). Both ends of the bleed close with a new `REASON_CATCHUP_COMPLETE`: `close_replayed_scopes` pops every replay-opened scope at the end of the catch-up pass (in a `finally`, so partial passes close too; deliberately unlocked, since a persona holds `_lock` across a whole event and taking it would stall boot), and `stale_close_reason` splits a **live** turn off a replay-opened scope at the seam that already owned wire rotation. A restart-interrupted conversation now drops the replayed span and keeps the live one, derived under its own principal. New counter `agent.interactions.closed.by_catchup_complete` meters the rate at which restart-window history is dropped. **Two costs, accepted and stated**: an interaction that closed entirely during downtime is no longer summarised at boot (a path already unreliable — no watermark, and `started_at` is boot time, not the wire timestamp); and the skip cannot distinguish "no principal because single-tenant" (where `'local'` is *correct*) from "no principal because replay lost it", since auth mode is not exposed to agents — so it fires under `disabled` too. Removing that second cost is ISSUE-0130 shape **(b)**, scheduled **v0.3.15**: persist `principal_id` on `messages` (channel store v11 → v12), stamp it server-side at publish, surface it on the history response, seed it in the replay builder — co-designed with R-2, which needs a column on the same table, so `messages` migrates once. *(Correction 2026-08-23: R-2's chosen shape is in-memory and adds no column, so the `messages` migration belongs to shape (b) alone and is not gated on R-2.)* **Withdrawn behaviour**: [RFC 0037](docs/rfcs/0037-memory-confidentiality-channel-classification.md) review item 8 pinned that a replayed rotation close stamped its episode with the channel classification; there is no row left to stamp, so those pins now assert the skip instead (live-path classification capture is untouched). Gate: `tests/unit/python/test_catchup_replay_principal_leak.py`.
- **`persatrix chat` could not authenticate at all under `auth.mode: enabled`** (found live at v0.3.14 release-prep PR 1, [F-1](docs/manual-tests/v0.3.14-execution-report.md#findings--follow-ups); fixed in that PR): every logged-in caller got `401 Unauthorized: authentication required` on the **primary CLI verb**, with a valid freshly-issued token on disk — the orchestrator rejected it at the middleware in 16 µs, before any handler. `main.rs` installs the bearer as a **default header on the shared client**, but `cmd_chat` builds its own long-timeout client for chat turns and never re-attached it, making the chat POST the one unauthenticated verb on the whole CLI surface — on exactly the surface v0.3.14 activates. No automated test covered the chat client's headers, and `MT-AUTH-001` exercises `chat` only under its `disabled` leg, where no header is needed. Fixed by extracting a pure `bearer_headers()` helper and feeding it into the chat client's builder. Gate: `cli/src/commands/chat_tests.rs` — token attaches, the credential is marked sensitive, a logged-out caller attaches nothing, and header-invalid bytes act logged-out rather than failing the command.

### 🧪 Testing

- **The v0.3.14 surface executed live end to end** — the [MT execution report](docs/manual-tests/v0.3.14-execution-report.md) ([#833](https://github.com/mkhomutov/Persatrix/pull/833)): the whole `MT-MEMORY-MULTIUSER-001` arc ran on a real provider under `auth.mode: enabled` with two bootstrapped accounts — four `Pass`, one `Pass (confound noted)`, one `Accepted-with-known-gap`, **zero `Fail`, zero `Pending`**. The release gate was deliberately *not* the green absence bar: an absence bar is satisfied by any empty recall, including a write that never landed, so the gate was the **two distinct emitted principal values read off `memory.db` and recorded verbatim** — `episodes [('alice-person', 2), ('bob-person', 1), ('local', 2)]`, `facts [('alice-person', 2), ('local', 2)]`, `relationships [('alice-person', 1), ('bob-person', 1), ('local', 2)]`. Both principals coexist in storage, which is the point: isolation is a **recall filter, not a delete**. Alice's disclosure landed under her own principal and survived Bob's turn and a persona restart (Leg 4 recalled *Mira* by name); Bob's turn recalled none of it, unconfounded. **Leg 5 (the activation-day reset) is `Accepted-with-known-gap`** — the storage claim proved decisively, but the behavioural bar is not observable in that room. The arc also produced both defects fixed in this release. Alongside: [MT-AUTH-001](docs/manual-tests/MT-AUTH-001.md) re-ran green as the auth substrate (bootstrap password floor, the anonymous `401`/`200` matrix with the deliberate agent-ingress carve-out, an 11th-login `429` carrying `Retry-After: 60`, the `0600` credential file, the checksum-chained audit trail, and a revoked token replayed on the wire answering `401`); `make eval-replay` **5/5** recipes; the Rust suite **293/0** at the RC tip and **297/0** after the F-1 regression tests; and the offline `make demo-autonomous` smoke at **$0**.

### ⬆️ Upgrade Notes

| Notable change | Detail |
|----------------|--------|
| **[No migrations — but NOT a transparent upgrade under `enabled`]** | **No store migrations this cycle.** Channel store stays v11, persona-memory at migration 17, both already at the v0.3.13 tag; nothing to run and no downgrade caution. **This is the one release where drop-in schema does not imply drop-in behaviour**: on a deployment already running `auth.mode: enabled`, every pre-existing row reads `'local'` and the strict-equality predicate has no carve-out, so each person's accumulated memory goes quiet the day emission lands. **A partition, not a deletion** — the corpus is still there under `'local'`. Remedies: accept it (each person rebuilds under their own principal — the intended end state); run single-tenant (`disabled`); or deliberately re-tag *only* if one account genuinely was the sole speaker. There is no automatic bridge **by design**. |
| **[Behaviour — the release]** memory is partitioned per authenticated person | Under `enabled`, the orchestrator emits a per-request principal derived from the RFC 0039 §F verified `participant_id` — the same value the chat surface stamps as `user_id`, so the tenant dimension and the conversation surface name one identity and an account rename never re-partitions anyone's memory. |
| **[Shared-room semantics]** | Two authenticated people in one channel get **per-speaker persona memory**: neither turn recalls the other's disclosures, including through the deliberately cross-room tiers (travelling facts, person identity). **Room continuity is unaffected** — transcript and verbatim history are *not* principal-scoped, so both people still see the same conversation. |
| **[Retired caveat]** | The v0.3.12/v0.3.13 "every caller resolves `local`" caveat is **retired** — but read it beside the scope bound below, so "memory is per-person" is not taken to cover close-time aggregates or agent-relayed cascades. |
| **[Scope bound — the boundary is per-TURN]** | A write *derived* from a turn does not always inherit the principal. **R-1 (aggregate close)**: the RFC 0020 interaction scope is the ROOM, not the speaker, so in a group channel every speaker's turns accumulate into one record whose close-time summary and extracted facts land under whichever principal closed it — and facts are cross-room by default. **R-2 (agent cascades)**: a persona's reply re-enters as a fresh unauthenticated publish, so orchestrator-mediated cascade turns dispatch under `'local'` (the in-process `EventDispatcher` cascade *does* forward the axis; only the REST hop loses it). Both **v0.3.15**. Neither is covered by `MT-MEMORY-MULTIUSER-001`, which drives a single-persona DM. |
| **[Behaviour]** a restart no longer derives memory from replayed history | Catch-up replay carries no principal, so a replayed span now derives **nothing** rather than filing one person's content under the shared `'local'` tenant ([ISSUE-0130](docs/issues/ISSUE-0130-catchup-replay-rederives-memory-under-default-principal.md)). **Cost, stated**: an interaction that closed entirely during downtime is no longer summarised at boot, and because auth mode is not exposed to agents the skip **also fires under `disabled`**, where `'local'` would have been correct. The persist-and-seed completion is **v0.3.15**. |
| **[Behaviour — agent traffic]** agent-origin turns stay `local` | Personas hold no accounts and drive the publish/convene REST seams anonymously (RFC 0039 §Non-Goals), so autonomous and agent-authored traffic writes `'local'` **by design**, even under `enabled`. |
| **[Behaviour — erasure]** `delete_by_subject` is principal-scoped | A caller now erases exactly the rows it could read, **counts included**. `session_id`/`epoch_id` stay unscoped by design (an erasure traversal must reach earlier rooms and runs). Cross-tenant erasure has no verb — [ISSUE-0127](docs/issues/ISSUE-0127-cross-principal-erasure-verb.md), owned by RFC 0013. |
| **[`auth.mode: disabled` is byte-identical]** | No header is emitted, every request resolves `'local'`, deterministic tests pin the no-delta — **including on the erasure path**. Single-tenant and CLI deployments see no change. |
| **[Fixed in prep]** `persatrix chat` under `enabled` | v0.3.13's CLI could not authenticate on the chat verb at all (`401` with a valid token) — the one unauthenticated verb on the surface. Fixed before the tag; no operator action. |
| **[Wire]** no protobuf changes | `persatrix-principal` is additive gRPC metadata on a rail the persona has bound since ISSUE-0081. A v0.3.13 persona under a v0.3.14 orchestrator isolates correctly; a v0.3.14 persona under a v0.3.13 orchestrator sees no header and resolves `'local'` — today's behaviour. REST unchanged. |
| **[Container]** rebuild the agent image | `Dockerfile.agent` now copies `schemas/` into site-packages for the RFC 0040 Phase 1 send-side validator. A stale image leaves it a silent no-op. |
| **[Dependencies]** | **No new direct dependencies in any ecosystem.** `make notices` produces no delta. |

## [0.3.13] - 2026-08-05

> **Codename:** Deferred calls closed

### ✨ Highlights

- **The v0.3.12 promise now holds everywhere it was made.** A persona's own memory-**tool** recalls obey the same per-request `--epoch`/`--session` isolation as injected memory — the scopes thread across the executor hop instead of falling back to the boot snapshot, and verbatim channel recall gains a foreign-epoch wall. The v0.3.12 Known Gap is closed and **proven live with the tool round evidenced in provenance**, not inferred from a green result.
- **A persona knows *who* it is talking to in a room it was never told.** The cross-room person-identity half of v0.3.12's memory work shipped a release ago but had never actually been run live; it ran this cycle and passed — and passed again on a **clean-room re-run** in a fresh, empty-transcript channel, where the persona's own memory is the only possible source of the name.
- **One channel's discussion length is tunable without retuning the fleet.** `max_cascade_depth` — post-ISSUE-0109 the de facto length knob for a productive autonomous discussion — is now declarable per channel, in config or at runtime, and binds exactly where the fleet value used to.
- **A stored fact subject can no longer place 120 characters of model-influenced text in the persona's own framing** — the facts-block header renders through a bounded template. Render-only: storage, fact rows, and recall matching are untouched, and every in-bounds subject renders byte-identically.
- **Drop-in upgrade.** No store migrations this cycle, in either store — and so, unlike v0.3.12, no downgrade caution.

### 🚀 Features

- **Per-channel `max_cascade_depth`** ([ISSUE-0114](docs/issues/ISSUE-0114-per-channel-cascade-depth-override.md), the v0.3.12 Known Gap): the RFC 0030 Layer 0 cascade-depth cap — post-ISSUE-0109 the de facto length knob for a productive autonomous discussion — is now declarable **per channel**, so one channel's discussions can be tuned without retuning the fleet. `channels[].max_cascade_depth` (zero/absent inherits the fleet cap; never config-disabled) resolves through a synchronized per-channel map read at the publish clamp, the fanout suppression, and the autonomous continuation's terminal-bound / §D structural close — the knob binds exactly where the fleet value used to. Runtime-editable per RFC 0050: it rides `ChannelConfigOverrides` (`PATCH …/config`, the web console's Channel settings panel), with conditional adopt/first-edit capture so an inheriting channel keeps tracking the fleet cap (and pre-v0.3.13 store rows keep their drift hashes). Alignment posture = the plan-locked **option (c)**: the Python dispatcher's defense-in-depth cap stays a per-process global backstop, so per-channel caps are validated `<=` the fleet cap at load (config-as-code), while a live edit above the fleet cap applies with a loud warning instead of a reject (the `SetEndVoteParams` posture — the fleet cap is startup-only, so a live edit is never bricked). Raising one channel past the fleet default means raising the fleet cap (and the aligned `agents/dispatch.py` pin) first.

### 🐛 Fixes

- **Per-request `--epoch`/`--session` isolation now holds at the memory-tool surface** ([ISSUE-0118](docs/issues/ISSUE-0118-tool-recall-bypasses-epoch-session-scopes.md), the v0.3.12 Known Gap — the F-3 leak class through the tool side door, found live at the fresh-epoch MT leg): the per-request epoch/session scopes now thread across the **executor hop** the same way acting classification does (RFC 0037 PR 7) — lifted structurally by `DispatchContext.for_event` off the same metadata keys the `on_event` binders read, re-entered around action processing (`request_scopes`) so executor-side memory seams (the end-vote close discharge, legacy cascade children, the chat surface's post-reply side-effects) resolve the request's world instead of construction snapshots — and the verbatim channel recall gains a **foreign-epoch wall**: the channel store is single-epoch by the ISSUE-0106(b) decision, so `recall_channel_messages` on a turn delivered under a different per-request epoch declines with an ordinary empty result (a fresh epoch sees nothing, and does not learn that withheld history exists). Deterministic CI pins the threading, the wall, the legacy-cascade seeding, and the injection/tool parity of the in-loop round; the live proof (the MT fresh-epoch leg with a tool round evidenced in provenance) was obtained at release-prep — see Testing below. `DispatchContext` + `wire_interaction_id` moved to `agents/dispatch_context.py` at the 500-line cap (re-exported — imports unchanged). Review hardening on the same rail: the recall wall's world epoch is captured env-only (`resolve_world_epoch_id` — an ambient scope at wiring time cannot poison the snapshot), the foreign-epoch decline logs at INFO (the only operator signal under an epoch misconfiguration; server-side only, the model still sees an ordinary empty result), and the **dormant principal axis threads through the same executor hop** (field + lift + re-entry + cascade seeding + chat-surface threading) so the v0.3.14 session-principal activation ([ISSUE-0082](docs/issues/ISSUE-0082-orchestrator-per-request-session-principal-emission.md) Part 2) inherits no executor-hop gap the day the rail lights up.
- **A stored fact subject can no longer place 120 chars of model-influenced text in the persona's own framing** ([ISSUE-0116](docs/issues/ISSUE-0116-fact-subject-renders-unquarantined.md), the v0.3.13 cuttable fold-in — direction 2 of the RFC 0026 topic amendment's named residual): the `Known facts about <subject>:` block header — rendered OUTSIDE the RFC 0009 `<external_data>` quarantine envelope, and reachable from any stimulus mentioning a stored topic subject since the topic-seeding widening — now renders the subject through a **bounded template** (`bounded_header_subject`: 4 words, then 48 chars over the survivors, any cut marked with the budget-truncator's `…`; `agents/persona_runtime/facts_render.py`, split from `facts_section.py` at the 500-line cap). Bounds are sized off the legitimate population (person display names, UUID participant ids, extractor-instructed short topic names), so in-bounds subjects render **byte-identically** and every landed header pin held without edits. **Header-only by design**: storage, the rendered `- subject predicate object` rows, subject grouping, and recall seeding keep the full canonical form, the helper is total (pre-amendment over-bound rows stay renderable and erasable), and the topic amendment's write-boundary invariants — `MAX_SUBJECT_CHARS`, control-character and envelope-delimiter rejection — are asserted unchanged, plus a drift pin that the render bound stays strictly tighter than the write cap. Honest residual, unchanged in kind: a short in-bounds imperative still renders verbatim — the template bounds the *surface*, not the semantics; subject namespaces with per-namespace validation (direction 3) remain with the future predicate registry.
- **The per-channel `max_cascade_depth` knob is reachable from the CLI, not only the web console** ([ISSUE-0114](docs/issues/ISSUE-0114-per-channel-cascade-depth-override.md); found live at v0.3.13 release-prep PR 1): the knob shipped on the server merge switch and the console's Channel settings panel but was never added to the CLI's flat-knob registry, so `persatrix channel config set <ch> max_cascade_depth=N` failed client-side with `unknown config knob` and `config get` omitted the row entirely — the one governance knob an operator most plausibly tunes per channel was web-only in practice. Added to `CONFIG_KNOBS` (the ninth flat knob), the typed config view (`#[serde(default)]`, so a pre-v0.3.13 orchestrator's payload still decodes), and the `config_rows` render map, which also carries it into `config export` / `diff`. **The lockstep guard that exists to catch exactly this was silently blind**: `cli_knob_set_matches_server_merge_switch` parses the flat knobs out of the Go source, and `mergeConfigPatch` moved to its own file at the ISSUE-0114 500-line-cap split — so the parse returned ZERO flat knobs while the existing `!server.is_empty()` assert stayed satisfied on the nested `reasoning.*` / `autonomous.*` knobs alone. The guard now reads the moved file and asserts the flat parse is non-empty, so a future move fails loudly instead of degrading to nested-only coverage. **And the guard was never run by CI**: the `rust` job was `cargo build` + `cargo clippy` only, leaving the whole 293-test Rust suite to the manual pre-tag sweep — `cargo test` is now a CI step, which is what turns both guards back into merge gates.

### 🧪 Testing

- **The v0.3.13 surface executed live end to end** — the [MT execution report](docs/manual-tests/v0.3.13-execution-report.md) ([#813](https://github.com/mkhomutov/Persatrix/pull/813)): the whole `MT-MEMORY-CROSSROOM-001` v1.1 arc ran on a real provider and passed on **all six legs, zero `Fail`** — including **Legs 1b/2b on their first-ever live run** ([ISSUE-0121](docs/issues/ISSUE-0121-crossroom-person-identity-legs-never-run-live.md)) and **Leg 4 with the memory-tool round evidenced in provenance** ([ISSUE-0118](docs/issues/ISSUE-0118-tool-recall-bypasses-epoch-session-scopes.md)). Two methodology points made the arc worth trusting: the in-room Leg 2b result was **confounded** by Leg 2's own reply naming the operator into the RFC 0034 conversation window — room-visible content, not memory — so it was re-run on a fresh empty-transcript channel (the identity-tier twin of the trap Leg 4 hit at v0.3.12, now written into the MT as a run rule); and the foreign-epoch decline line is emitted from **inside the tool body**, which is what makes Leg 4 proof of a tool round rather than an inference from call counts (tool execution itself logs at DEBUG, and the `relationship` tier emits no provenance at all). The ISSUE-0114 cascade arc passed all five checks — resolution, the option-(c) above-fleet warn-never-brick posture, and the bound firing at the **channel's own** value at runtime while the fleet default bound elsewhere. Continuity bar `make eval-replay` **5/5**; the offline `make demo-autonomous` smoke green at **$0**; whole live phase **25 wallet leases / $0.3038**. **Three findings were fixed in-PR, zero deferred** (the CLI knob surface, its silently-blind lockstep guard, and `cargo test` missing from CI — see the Fixes above — plus the MT's own Leg 2b run rule).

### ⬆️ Upgrade Notes

| Notable change | Detail |
|----------------|--------|
| **[No migrations — the upgrade is drop-in]** | This cycle ships **no store migrations**: the channel store stays at **v11** and the persona-memory store at **v17**, both already at the v0.3.12 tag. Nothing to run and nothing to time — and, unlike v0.3.12, **no downgrade caution**: a v0.3.13 store still opens under a v0.3.12 binary. |
| **[Behaviour — the headline]** memory tools obey `--epoch` / `--session` | The v0.3.12 **[Known gap]** row for `--epoch`/`--session` with memory tools is **retired**: the per-request scopes now thread across the executor hop, so an agent-elected memory-**tool** recall resolves the request's world instead of the construction snapshot — run isolation can now be relied on for tool-bearing personas. **Expect less, not more**: a tool recall that previously surfaced foreign-epoch rows now excludes them, and `recall_channel_messages` on a turn delivered under a different per-request epoch declines with an ordinary empty result (the channel store is single-epoch by the [ISSUE-0106](docs/issues/ISSUE-0106-recall-epoch-filter-decoupled-from-unpersisted-publish-epoch.md)(b) decision). That is the fix, not a regression — a fresh epoch sees nothing, and does not learn that withheld history exists. **Operators who unknowingly depended on the leak will see empty results**: a probe run under a throwaway `--epoch` that previously still saw live history now correctly sees none. The only operator signal is server-side — the decline logs at **INFO**. |
| **[New knob]** per-channel `max_cascade_depth` | Declare it per channel in `config/channels.yaml` (`channels[].max_cascade_depth`; zero or absent inherits the fleet cap) or edit it at runtime — `PATCH /api/v1/channels/{id}/config`, `persatrix channel config set <channel> max_cascade_depth=N`, or the console's Channel settings panel. **Raising one channel past the fleet default means raising the fleet cap — and the aligned `agents/dispatch.py` backstop — first**: the Python dispatcher's per-process cap is defense-in-depth and suppresses dispatches first, so the extra depth is otherwise unreachable. Config-as-code **rejects** an above-fleet value at load; a **live** edit above the fleet cap **applies with a loud WARN** carrying a `remedy` field rather than being rejected (the `SetEndVoteParams` posture — the fleet cap is startup-only, so a live edit is never bricked). An inheriting channel keeps tracking the fleet cap, and pre-v0.3.13 store rows keep their drift hashes. |
| **[Behaviour — render-only]** bounded facts-header subject | The `Known facts about <subject>:` header — which renders outside the RFC 0009 `<external_data>` quarantine envelope — now passes the subject through a bounded template (4 words, then 48 characters over the survivors, any cut marked `…`). **Storage, the rendered `- subject predicate object` rows, subject grouping, and recall matching are unchanged.** Bounds are sized off the legitimate population (person display names, UUID participant ids, extractor-instructed short topic names), so an in-bounds subject renders **byte-identically**; only an over-long or many-worded subject renders differently, and only in that one header line. |
| **[Wire]** no protobuf changes | No new or changed proto fields this cycle — a v0.3.13 orchestrator and a v0.3.12 persona fleet interoperate on the wire unchanged. ISSUE-0118's fix threads the scopes **internally** (`event.metadata` → `DispatchContext.for_event` → the executor task), and the REST surface gains only the additive per-channel knob on `PATCH …/config`, so old clients are unaffected. |
| **[Dependencies]** | **None.** No new or changed direct dependencies in any language this cycle; `cargo update --workspace` re-locked only the workspace crate's own version string. |
| **[Known gap]** person-identity **capture** is model-elected | The live legs prove cross-room identity **recall**. **Capture** still depends on the persona electing `store_note(contact:<id>)` during the introduction, and lands only on the id the note is keyed to — there is no deterministic capture path. It captured cleanly on the first live attempt, so none was scoped forward; recorded as a finding instead. |

## [0.3.12] - 2026-08-02

> **Codename:** Memory that travels

### ✨ Highlights

- **A persona's knowledge now follows it into every room it belongs to.** What it learned in a DM is available in the standup — consolidated `topic.*` facts cross rooms outright, raw experience is room-first *ranked* rather than room-walled — so the same persona stops being a stranger in half the places you talk to it. The widening shipped **shadow-first and was promoted on a measured, reproducible verdict**, not a judgement call.
- **What makes that safe is classification, not walls.** Every room carries a confidentiality level (`public` < `internal` < `restricted` < `secret`, defaulting to `internal`), every memory entry is stamped with the level of the room it was learned in, and a deterministic gate withholds anything stamped above the room the persona is currently acting in — on the memory-injection path, on the channel-recall path, and at the memory tools. Below the stamp a persona is **informed by, but does not disclose**: a protected memory surfaces as a one-line declassified restatement in its own relevance position, and verbatim again back at its own level.
- **Verified accounts, opt-in** — password login, revocable sessions, an operator/user role gate, and a verified `participant_id` claim so the orchestrator acts as *who you are* rather than who the request body claims. `auth.mode` ships **`disabled`**: unchanged behaviour byte-for-byte until an operator turns it on.
- Proven live at release-prep on a real provider across two rooms, plus the six-leg enabled-mode auth matrix in a real browser ([execution report](docs/manual-tests/v0.3.12-execution-report.md)).

### 🚀 Features

- **RFC 0037 PR 1 — the §A classification lattice + channel classification at rest (ships dark).** The foundation of the v0.3.12 memory-confidentiality boundary: the fixed four-level lattice (`public` < `internal` < `restricted` < `secret`) lands as twin canonical rank helpers (`internal/channels/classification.go` + `agents/persona_runtime/classification.py` — no code path compares level strings directly), with fail-closed split into the **three §A directions** and each owned by exactly one named resolver: stamping defaults to `internal` (a channel nobody classified is confidential-by-default), an unknown *acting* level floors to `public` (inject/return less), and an unknown *entry* label is withheld outright — a single blanket default could satisfy at most one of these. Every resolver is pure and pinned silent on both sides: rule (c)'s "and logged" half is the gate-side caller's, since only it holds the entry identity that makes the line triageable and the §F filter evaluates one entry per candidate row. The one-resolver-per-rule discipline is structural rather than advisory — the core lookup is private on both sides (unexported `classificationRank`, `_classification_rank`), so a gate author reaches only the three rule-named doors and has no unnamed lookup to open-code a locally chosen default against. The schema enum is the vocabulary's third copy and is now pinned to the runtime table rather than to a literal of its own, so a level added to the lattice without a schema edit fails the config-schema suite. Channel classification exists at rest: `config/channels.yaml` + the schema gain the per-channel `classification` field and the fleet-wide `dm_default_classification` knob (absent → `internal`; unknown levels rejected at both `make validate` and the Go loader), channel-store migration **v11** adds `channels.classification` with an `internal` backfill, and `GetOrCreateDM` stamps the DM knob at creation (an existing DM is never re-stamped by a lookup). The item-8 **dark-window rule is enforced, not documented**: a declared level above `internal` is rejected at startup (`ErrClassificationAboveDarkWindow`, removed when the §D gate arms) — during the window a group declaration does not even reach the store row, so a `restricted` channel would read as a boundary to the operator while every path still saw `internal`, which is worse than no declaration at all. **Dark substrate:** nothing reads the column yet — the wire lift is PR 2 and the §D hard gate / §F recall filter are PRs 4–5 — so runtime behaviour is byte-for-byte unchanged, and the item-8 dark-window rule holds: classify nothing above `internal` until the full Phase-1 set ships. ([RFC 0037](docs/rfcs/0037-memory-confidentiality-channel-classification.md); [PR plan](docs/rfcs/0037-pr-plan.md); [v0.3.12 plan](docs/v0.3.12-plan.md))
- **RFC 0037 PR 2 — classification on the wire (ships dark).** The §B two-path wire contract: every delivered channel event now carries the acting channel's §A confidentiality level so the persona runtime can run the (PR 4) §D injection gate per turn without a channel-metadata roundtrip. `ChannelMessageEvent` gains `classification = 31` (regenerated with the CI-pinned protoc 34.1 / protoc-gen-go v1.36.11 / protoc-gen-go-grpc v1.6.1), populated from the `channels` row per dispatch through a router-side read-through cache (`classificationFor`) so the post-commit dispatch path stays I/O-free after a channel's first resolve — a failed row read rides the wire as *empty*, which the receiver resolves to the `public` acting floor (§A rule (b)), never `internal`, so a store hiccup can only under-inject. The REST leg feeds on-startup catch-up replay: the channel object (`channelResponse`) and the history/thread envelopes (`historyResponse`) carry the same channel-level `classification`. The PR 1 note's deferred `Channel` plumbing lands — `CreateChannel`/`CreateChannelWithMembers` persist the field (absent normalizes to `internal` at the store boundary, the §A rule-(a) SessionID-rewrite precedent), `GetChannel`/`ListChannels`/`GetOrCreateDM` scan it back verbatim — and so does the plan's "existing rows" adoption debt: the new `ChannelStore.SetChannelClassification` (strict on the level — an UPDATE holds an explicit operator value, so a typo is rejected, not normalized) plus a `ReconcileConfig` adoption arm that converges a pre-declaration row (the v11 `internal` backfill) onto the declared level at boot and refreshes the dispatch cache, with undeclared store channels untouched (§B coexistence) — the arm normalizes the declared level first, since the rule-(a) fill lives in `LoadConfig` rather than `Validate` and a Config built in code would otherwise fail the strict UPDATE and abort startup. The cache's other coherence hook is deletion: `DELETE /api/v1/channels/{id}` writes store-direct, so the handler calls the new `ChannelRouter.ForgetChannelClassification` beside its existing `PurgeChannelInteraction` — without it a re-created channel id keeps dispatching the deleted channel's cached level, the single ordering in which this cache can *under*-classify (a cached `public` over a re-created `internal` row) rather than over-classify. Python-side, both ingress paths seed one `channel_classification` metadata key through the new `agents/channel_event_classification.py` seam — the live gRPC lift (`seed_wire_metadata`) off the typed field, catch-up replay (`_build_replay_event`) off the channel-list JSON — verbatim-or-nothing (no allowlist: §A rule (b) belongs to `acting_rank` alone, the PR 1 one-resolver-per-rule discipline; garbage floors at read, and only a byte bound guards the metadata bag), with the one tolerant reader `wire_channel_classification` returning `None` for the unclassified-legacy shape the rule-(b) floor consumes. **Dark**: the value is delivered on both paths and read by nothing until the PR 4 gate arms. (RFC 0037 §B; v0.3.12 plan Phase 1.)
- **RFC 0037 PR 3 — the memory substrate: protection levels at rest (ships dark).** The §C provenance/protection shape lands on the persona-memory store: migration **v16** adds `protection_level` (NOT NULL DEFAULT `internal` — which IS the §C backfill: neither silently `public`, a disclosure, nor silently `secret`, which would withhold a persona's entire history from itself; notes are the documented honest exception, backfilling `internal` regardless of where they were authored), nullable `source_channel_id`, and nullable `provenance_json` (the multi-source shape created now so the RFC 0049 v0.4.0 cross-scope pump needs no second migration) to the **episodes / facts / notes** tiers, plus the §E `memory_projections` table (written from PR 6 on, keyed `(entry_id, entry_tier, level)`, carrying the RFC 0008 §H `agent_id` ACL axis off the key — personas share one SQLite file, foreign keys are off, and PR 6's parent-delete/erasure cleanup runs after the parent row is gone, so the owner must be on the projection row and cannot be added later without a v17). The capture point is the interaction record, per §C's "classification is read once per interaction": the `Interaction` freezes the acting channel's wire classification + channel id **at open** (`add_turn`/`start` honour the pair only when they open the record — the `session_id` sibling-mislabel precedent), held VERBATIM so the §A rule-(a) coercion happens exactly once, at the stamp sites — the Phase-1 closing-row insert (`close_path.py`) and the facts-extraction dispatch (`fact_extractor.py`) both stamp through `normalize_for_stamp` (absent/unknown → `internal`, never `public`), so tick/legacy/garbage captures label confidential-by-default while a mislabeled stored row still fails closed at read time (rule (c) withholds unknown entry labels). Supersession restamps by construction (a superseding fact is a new row stamped from its own source — §C item 3, pinned by test) and reinforcement never touches the column. The §C ≤-`internal` **identity write-through rule** arms beside it: the cross-room relationship identity tier carries no protection level (an ungated egress surface — a role learned in a `secret` channel would render into every room's prompt), so `store_note(contact:<id>)` routes to the room-scoped note instead of the identity tier whenever the acting classification outranks `internal`, reading the acting level off the new task-local `agents/acting_classification.py` axis (the `sender_type` twin, bound as the fifth `request_scope_from_metadata` axis from the PR 2 wire seed; unbound floors to `public` per rule (b), keeping every pre-classification path byte-for-byte). **Dark**: stamped, never read — nothing consumes `protection_level` until the PR 4 §D gate, and the PR 1 dark-window guard still rejects any channel classified above `internal`, so the write-through branch cannot fire in production. (RFC 0037 §C/§E; v0.3.12 plan Phase 1.)
- **RFC 0037 PR 4 — the §D hard gate: the confidentiality boundary arms (and the dark window closes).** Every channel-derived memory tier — channel history, episodic recall, facts, notes — is now rank-filtered against the turn's **acting classification** *before* the RFC 0017 budget (`agents/persona_runtime/injection_gate.py`, wired into `_inject_memory_context`): an entry stamped above the room the persona is acting in is withheld outright, so a `restricted` DM memory cannot reach an `internal` standup's prompt (withhold-only here; PR 6 adds the projection branch). The acting level resolves by **positive-list event class** — channel-anchored types read the PR 2 wire stamp, tick-shaped types floor to `public` (§A rule (b)) — and a positive-list test forces a conscious choice for every future `EventType` rather than letting a new event class inherit a silent default. Rule (c)'s "and logged" half lands here as one aggregated WARNING per turn, naming each unknown-labeled entry, its channel, and the acting level. The **notes leg becomes sound end to end**: `store_note` stamps rule (a) from the task-local acting level, `recall_notes` carries a non-optional injectable-level `IN` predicate at the query (a corrupted label falls out in SQL, and withheld rows never burn limit slots), and `update_note` re-stamps `max(existing, acting)` through a raise-only CASE. The RFC 0038 §B **single-channel-turn guard** rides in (Decision #3): a channel-anchored turn whose `SEND_CHANNEL_MESSAGE` targets a *foreign* channel becomes `DO_NOTHING` + WARNING — the gate reasons about exactly one acting level per turn, so a turn that publishes elsewhere would step around it. Operators inherit the one-time `PERSATRIX_NOTES_BACKFILL_PROTECTION_LEVEL` flag on the v16 notes arm ([ISSUE-0115](docs/issues/ISSUE-0115-rfc0037-section-c-stamping-residuals.md)(a)): honoured only at the migration moment, vocabulary-checked (an invalid value fails the migration loudly), inert afterwards. **The PR 1 dark window is over** — the startup rejection of channels declared above `internal` is deleted exactly per its removal note, so from v0.3.12 an operator may classify a room `restricted` or `secret` and have every path honour it. ([RFC 0037 §§A–D/§H](docs/rfcs/0037-memory-confidentiality-channel-classification.md); [RFC 0038 §B](docs/rfcs/0038-concurrent-context-awareness-relay.md); v0.3.12 plan Phase 1.)
- **RFC 0037 PR 5 — the §F recall filter: classification reaches the channel store (the last verbatim egress path closes).** `RecallMessages` — the recall surface that reads the channel store directly, *past* the §D memory gate — now composes a **non-optional classification predicate** with the RFC 0036 membership join: a message is servable only when its channel's classification ranks at or below the acting level, so `secret` room text can no longer be recalled into a `public` turn. The §A ordering reaches SQL as an `IN` predicate over the new Go `InjectableLevels` resolver (the twin of PR 4's `injectable_levels`, keeping the rank table single-sourced per language); a corrupted label falls out of the set (rule (c)) and a missing channel row fails the `EXISTS`. The clause rides the scope string, leaving the shared membership/epoch fragment byte-identical. The endpoint's `acting_classification` is **required and vocabulary-validated** — silently flooring a pre-§F caller to `public` would return empty everywhere on an `internal`-default store — while the store floors defensively underneath (rule (b)) and the audit detail records the level; the memory tool binds it from the turn's task-local classification seam, so the level is never an LLM parameter (an unbound tick floors to `public`, a garbage wire stamp floors rather than `400`s). **One deliberate break** ([ISSUE-0106](docs/issues/ISSUE-0106-recall-epoch-filter-decoupled-from-unpersisted-publish-epoch.md), direction (b)): the recall **`epoch_id` body override is removed** — any presence, `"live"` and `""` included, is now a pointed `400`, because silently accepting it implied an isolation axis that does not exist (separate runs never share a store). ([RFC 0037 §F](docs/rfcs/0037-memory-confidentiality-channel-classification.md); [PR plan](docs/rfcs/0037-pr-plan.md); v0.3.12 plan Phase 1.)
- **RFC 0037 PR 6 — declassification projections: the §D gate stops being blunt (Phase 2, "informed by, doesn't disclose").** A persona whose protected memory is withheld in a lower-classified room is no longer amnesiac about it: when a **protected** interaction closes (`restricted`/`secret` — the operator opt-in class; the universal `internal` default is deliberately excluded, so every ordinary close keeps its exact prior prompt bytes and all four RFC 0044 goldens replay with no re-record), the RFC 0020 close-consolidation call is asked — same call, one extra structured field — for a **one-line declassified restatement at each lower level**, persisted into the PR 3 `memory_projections` table keyed by interaction id (a re-consolidation replaces, never accumulates). At injection time the §D gate's new projection branch (`projection_branch.py`, over the gate's per-tier decision record) serves a withheld episode's **highest projection `≤ L`** in the withheld original's own relevance position — a `restricted` "sunset REDWOLF next quarter" memory surfaces in a `public` room as "a quarterly roadmap decision was made", verbatim again when acting back at `restricted` — across both episode-backed tiers (episodic recall + channel history; `fact`/`note` projections await their RFC 0027 reflection producer). The §E **honest boundary** is enforced structurally: the deterministic guarantee stays §D's verbatim withhold (a served projection is best-effort, its parse filters out any model-hallucinated level at or above the interaction's own stamp, and every failure — malformed half, write error, read error — degrades to the Phase-1 blunt withhold, never fails the summary or the turn), the §G manifest labels a served projection at the *projection's* level, and the verbatim-withheld counts the RFC 0049 shadow traces read stay untouched. ([RFC 0037 §E](docs/rfcs/0037-memory-confidentiality-channel-classification.md#e-declassification-projections); [PR plan](docs/rfcs/0037-pr-plan.md); v0.3.12 plan Phase 1.)
- **RFC 0037 PR 7 — the §G leak tripwire: verbatim leaks become observable (Phase 3, "a smoke detector, not a lock").** §D/§F close the verbatim disclosure paths deterministically; this arms the backstop for what they cannot prevent — protected text reaching an outgoing message anyway (a mis-stamped entry, a §E projection that copied source text verbatim, a missed injection path). Each turn whose §D gate **withheld** entries now stamps a hash-only `TripwireWatch` onto its event — per withheld entry: tier, id, protection level (rule-(c) corrupted labels ride as the sentinel `unknown`, never the raw string), and SHA-256 hashes of every rolling **8-normalized-word span** of its text (casefold + word tokenization; the §G OQ-5 conservative threshold, tuned only once real telemetry exists) — which `DispatchContext.for_event` lifts structurally to the `ActionExecutor`. There, every outgoing `SEND_CHANNEL_MESSAGE` is checked for a shared span: on a hit, a **metadata-only** `channel.confidentiality_tripwire` RFC 0009 audit record (persona, target channel, entry tier/id/level, acting classification, match count — never the text; Go-side constant reserved **security-class**, drift-pinned) plus the new `channel.confidentiality.tripwire_hits{tier, protection_level}` metric. The message is **never blocked** (blocking is RFC 0012's enforced egress gate) and the whole check is exception-proof — a poisoned watch degrades to silence around an unchanged publish. Watching the *withheld* set (not the injected manifest) is what makes a hit meaningful: an admitted entry's level never exceeds the §B-guarded publish target, so under §D a true hit always indicates a bug worth surfacing — and it is also why the common case costs nothing (nothing withheld → no hashing, no watch, no work). ([RFC 0037 §G](docs/rfcs/0037-memory-confidentiality-channel-classification.md#g-the-leak-tripwire); [PR plan](docs/rfcs/0037-pr-plan.md); v0.3.12 plan Phase 1.)
- **RFC 0049 PR 1 — the `topic.*` capture path (the 0026 topic-predicate amendment lands).** The scenario-2 capture half: the frozen fact-predicate allowlist gains the **closed** `topic.*` class (`topic.has_status` / `topic.has_deadline` / `topic.decided` / `topic.owned_by` — a new verb stays a deliberate amendment + PR, and the `topic.` prefix grants no free pass), the combined summarize+extract prompt now proposes canonical short topic subjects alongside self/counterparty, and fact-recall seeding widens beyond self+sender: `FactStore.topic_subjects()` enumerates the store's distinct live topic subjects (bounded, most-recent-first, scoped exactly like `recall` — capture widens, *scope* does not; the cross-room L2 widening is PR 2, still behind the RFC 0037 gate) and the new `topic_seeds.py` seeds recall for the ≤3 subjects the inbound stimulus mentions (deterministic word-boundary match, no LLM in the read path; TICK events still issue zero DB round-trips). The named **allowlist blast-radius re-review** ran and its bounds shipped at the storage boundary: `MAX_SUBJECT_CHARS=120` post-normalization, `MAX_OBJECT_CHARS=400` via the new `validate_object`, and both fields reject RFC 0009 `<external_data>` delimiter escapes — recorded in the [0026 amendment](docs/rfcs/0026-amendment-topic-subject-predicates.md). Protection stamping is inherited unchanged (a topic fact from a `restricted` DM is stamped `restricted`, pinned by test); the two RFC 0044 goldens re-recorded offline (the widened predicate list shifts every close-path request hash) and replay green. (RFC 0049 Phase 1 / RFC 0026 topic amendment; v0.3.12 plan Phase 1b.)
- **RFC 0049 PR 2 — L2 cross-room fact recall, in SHADOW (the 0031 fact-scope amendment lands).** The scenario-2 *scope* half: fact recall's room wall — the RFC 0031 §D `session_id` filter, which per-`(agent, channel)` session binding makes the room wall for channel turns — is dropped in **shadow**. Each sender-bearing turn, the new `facts_shadow.py` pass re-derives the live seed set (person seeds every-predicate, topic seeds `TOPIC_PREDICATES`-scoped — the PR 1 reachability bound holds) with both the topic enumeration and the per-seed recall widened to all sessions, takes the **cross-room delta** (widened rows the live recall didn't return), runs it through a dedicated RFC 0037 §D gate at the turn's acting classification, and records one structured log trace of what *would* have been injected — **nothing enters the live prompt**: no WorkingMemory section, no RFC 0017 budget charge, no §G manifest entry, no reinforcement write, and both RFC 0044 goldens replay byte-identically. The trace names ids/subjects/levels/provenance but never the fact object (the log is its own egress surface); `epoch`/`principal` stay strict-equality walls on the widened read; a `restricted` fact on an `internal`-acting turn is a withheld count, never a candidate. Operators steer via `memory.facts.cross_room: off|shadow` (default `shadow`; `live` is rejected until the PR 4 promotion). The RFC 0044 harness records the traces (`EvalRun.shadow_traces` → a `shadow_traces` report-artifact key) — the read surface for PR 4's golden-trace shadow→live measurement gate. (RFC 0049 Phase 1 / RFC 0031 fact-scope amendment; v0.3.12 plan Phase 1b.)
- **RFC 0049 PR 3 — L1 room-first ranking, in SHADOW (the L1 cross-room amendment lands).** Raw episodic recall's room wall — the RFC 0031 §D session filter — becomes a **ranking cue**, in shadow: the new room-ranked recall mode (`agents/memory/episodic_room_ranked.py`) widens the session axis and multiplies same-room rows' composite score by `ROOM_BOOST_FACTOR=2.0` (`session_boost_expr`, all three query branches: FTS5/LIKE/recency), so same-room episodes lead at equal relevance — the dementia-test continuity bar preserved as a *ranking* property — while a clearly better other-room episode remains admissible. Each channel-anchored turn, the new `episodes_shadow.py` pass re-runs the live episodic recall through that mode (same query/limit/`min_score`), takes the **cross-room delta**, runs it through a dedicated RFC 0037 §D gate at the turn's acting classification, and records one structured log trace with each candidate's widened-`rank` displacement position — **nothing enters the live prompt** (the live path keeps `sessions=None` — the wall — until the PR 4 flip). The widened read is **side-effect-free** (no `access_count` bump — a reinforcing shadow would perturb live ranking and shift the landed goldens off their cassettes); the trace never carries the episode summary (log-egress bound); `epoch`/`principal` stay strict-equality walls; tick-shaped turns skip the pass (zero idle DB round-trips). Operators steer via `memory.episodic.cross_room: off|shadow` (default `shadow`; `live` rejected until PR 4). The RFC 0044 harness now captures **both** shadow streams (L2 facts + L1 episodes) into one `tier`-keyed `shadow_traces` artifact — the PR 4 measurement's read surface; both goldens replay byte-identically. (RFC 0049 Phase 1 / the RFC 0049 L1 amendment; v0.3.12 plan Phase 1b.)
