The `ibm-watsonx-ai` SDK ships as a pyproject **extra** (RFC 0053 OQ #4), so a single-provider deployment carries no new runtime dependency; `make demo-watsonx` (+ `docker-compose.watsonx.yaml`, priced `meta-llama/llama-3-3-70b-instruct` / `ibm/granite-3-8b-instruct` aliases) installs it into the agent image via the `AGENT_EXTRAS` build arg (so `--build` is required) and plumbs the secret `WATSONX_API_KEY` plus the non-secret `WATSONX_PROJECT_ID`/`SPACE_ID`/`URL` env fallbacks (the mounted alias `provider_config` stays the source of truth for `project_id`/`url` — the RFC 0053 §E secret/config split). The `WATSONX_API_KEY=…` assignment joins the RFC 0009 secret-redactor allow-list as a named `watsonx-api-key` pattern registered before the generic fallback (IBM Cloud IAM keys have no distinctive standalone prefix, so — unlike Google's `AIza…` — the redactor pins the assignment surface rather than shape-matching a bare key). `MT-PROVIDER-WATSONX-001` documents the live single-provider smoke (`gen_ai.system=watsonx`); the four-vendor headline (`MT-AUTONOMOUS-MULTIPROVIDER-001`) is the cuttable RFC 0052 PR 9. ([RFC 0053](docs/rfcs/0053-gemini-watsonx-providers.md); [PR plan](docs/rfcs/0053-pr-plan.md); [MT-PROVIDER-WATSONX-001](docs/manual-tests/MT-PROVIDER-WATSONX-001.md))
- **RFC 0052 PR 8 (Phase 4a) — `make demo-autonomous`: the one-command offline autonomous brainstorm, zero keys and zero spend.** The adoption demo for the human-free channel. It boots the society on the zero-cost `mock` provider (the RFC 0033 offline alias, priced at $0), **arms** the bundled `roundtable` channel — a curated 3-persona roster that ships *disarmed* in `config/channels.yaml` for default-deploy safety — and **convenes** it, so the whole arc runs with **no API key, no network, and no human turn**: `nova-sparrow` (the convener) opens the "Should we adopt a monorepo?" topic, `ember-owl` and `iron-fox` discuss it through the ordinary governed wake chain, and the chair `ember-owl` closes with a synthesized recommendation delivered to every member. The demo arms + convenes at **runtime** (the exact `MT-AUTONOMOUS-001` operator flow — arm via the CLI, convene via the CLI), so the bundled config stays byte-for-byte **safe at rest**; the discussion is driven by curated fixtures in `config/offline_responses.yaml`, so it is deterministic — it demonstrates the *shape* of a human-free brainstorm, not a live model's reasoning. A new offline smoke ([`tests/integration/test_autonomous_offline_smoke.py`](tests/integration/test_autonomous_offline_smoke.py)) pins the checklist contract in CI: the curated `mock` replies produce a **non-empty, on-topic synthesis** for the shipped `roundtable` topic/goal at $0, and the demo roster stays convene-eligible (a config-drift guard). The curated roster is also captured as a design blueprint (`blueprints/autonomous-roundtable/`). The four-vendor headline (the same channel on Anthropic + OpenAI + Gemini + watsonx) is the cuttable PR 9, gated on RFC 0053. ([RFC 0052](docs/rfcs/0052-autonomous-agent-channels.md); [PR plan](docs/rfcs/0052-pr-plan.md); [MT-AUTONOMOUS-001](docs/manual-tests/MT-AUTONOMOUS-001.md))
- **RFC 0053 PR 3 (closeout) — the combined `providers` extra + provider-guide finalize; RFC 0053 → ✅ Implemented.** The four-cloud-vendor provider roster (Anthropic + OpenAI + Gemini + watsonx.ai) that RFC 0052's flagship human-free brainstorm exists to enable is complete, so this slice packages and closes it out — **no new provider code**. `agents/pyproject.toml` gains a combined **`providers`** extra (`pip install 'persatrix-agents[providers]'`) that pulls **both** optional provider SDKs in one step instead of naming each (`[gemini,watsonx]`) — `anthropic` / `openai` are always-on base dependencies and Ollama reuses the base `openai` SDK, so only the two RFC 0053 clouds carry an extra and the bundle is exactly `google-genai` + `ibm-watsonx-ai`. The combined bundle is a **self-referencing extra** (`Persatrix-agents[gemini,watsonx]`) that re-exports the individual provider extras by name rather than duplicating their pins, so a bumped SDK pin is inherited automatically and can never drift; a drift guard ([`tests/unit/python/test_pyproject_provider_extras.py`](tests/unit/python/test_pyproject_provider_extras.py)) asserts the self-reference names every individual provider extra, so a future provider SDK not folded into `providers` fails CI rather than shipping a combined extra that silently omits a vendor. The [model-providers guide](docs/guides/model-providers.md) documents the combined extra and the four-cloud-vendor install path, and hands the cross-vendor **blueprint** + the live `MT-AUTONOMOUS-MULTIPROVIDER-001` (the four-vendor headline) off to the cuttable [RFC 0052 PR 9](docs/rfcs/0052-pr-plan.md), where they live. RFC 0053's status flips to **✅ Implemented** (all three PRs: [#731](https://github.com/mkhomutov/Persatrix/pull/731) Gemini · [#732](https://github.com/mkhomutov/Persatrix/pull/732) watsonx · this closeout). ([RFC 0053](docs/rfcs/0053-gemini-watsonx-providers.md); [PR plan](docs/rfcs/0053-pr-plan.md))
- **RFC 0052 PR 9 (Phase 4b, closeout) — the four-vendor headline + RFC 0052 → ✅ Implemented.** The v0.3.11 flagship, and the RFC's closeout. Because RFC 0053 landed (Gemini [#731](https://github.com/mkhomutov/Persatrix/pull/731) + watsonx [#732](https://github.com/mkhomutov/Persatrix/pull/732)), the cross-vendor leg is **not** cut: this PR lands the full headline. A new **four-vendor blueprint** ([`blueprints/autonomous-multivendor/blueprint.yaml`](blueprints/autonomous-multivendor/blueprint.yaml)) captures the demo's most vivid form — **four personas, each pinned by an RFC 0033 model alias to a *different* cloud vendor** (`nova-sparrow`→Anthropic convener, `ember-owl`→OpenAI chair, `iron-fox`→Gemini, `slate-heron`→watsonx.ai), brainstorming one topic in one channel **with no human**, under a **single shared** per-interaction cost cap (not a per-seat cap). It is the vivid proof that the conversation layer is provider-agnostic (RFC 0033 §H — the governed wake chain carries a discussion whose four voices are four vendors' models, and nothing in the channel/governance/wallet layer knows or cares), the cross-vendor sibling of PR 8's offline `mock`-mapped `autonomous-roundtable`. The blueprint is **self-contained** — it carries its own `model_aliases` block (the `optimization.yaml` `models.aliases` shape) so the four-vendor mapping is legible in one file and, crucially, **resolvable**: the TDD guard ([`tests/unit/python/test_autonomous_multivendor_blueprint.py`](tests/unit/python/test_autonomous_multivendor_blueprint.py)) runs each seat's alias through the **real** RFC 0033 resolver and pins that the four providers are exactly `{anthropic, openai, gemini, watsonx}` (four distinct cloud vendors, no local `mock`/`ollama` seat), that every seat alias is **priced fail-closed** (an unpriced non-local alias would silently zero that vendor's RFC 0023 budget gate — the guard `SystemExit`s first), and that the roster is a well-formed **agent-only, capped** autonomous channel (convener ≠ chair per OQ #1, every `extends` anchor resolving to `templates/personas.yaml` — the PR #729 dangling-anchor lesson, no human member). `MT-AUTONOMOUS-MULTIPROVIDER-001` documents the live headline — all four vendors keyed, per-seat vendor routing confirmed on the `gen_ai.system` telemetry, converge + synthesize with no human, total spend ≤ the single shared cap — with the live run scheduled for release-prep. **RFC 0052 flips to ✅ Implemented** (all four phases across PRs 1–9); the OQ #5 defaults-calibration follow-up is filed as [ISSUE-0109](docs/issues/ISSUE-0109-rfc0052-autonomous-defaults-calibration.md) (tune `max_rounds` / cap / the `1+N` reserve fraction / the standing aggregate bound after the live soak). ([RFC 0052](docs/rfcs/0052-autonomous-agent-channels.md); [PR plan](docs/rfcs/0052-pr-plan.md); [MT-AUTONOMOUS-MULTIPROVIDER-001](docs/manual-tests/MT-AUTONOMOUS-MULTIPROVIDER-001.md))

### 🐛 Fixes

- **RFC 0052 — the convene / synthesis forced turns are now actually deliverable to real agents (a convened channel silently produced nothing).** Booting the offline demo end-to-end surfaced that the whole autonomous arc — convene opener, §D synthesis turn, and the PR 6 anti-collapse advance turn — could not deliver to real gRPC agents, because the **synthetic dispatch sender ids** (`orchestrator:convene` / `orchestrator:synthesis`) — deliberately carrying a reserved `:` so they can never collide with a real participant id — were rejected at **two** participant-id validation layers the in-process tests never exercised (the Go acceptance suite mocks the dispatcher; the live `MT-AUTONOMOUS-*` runs were pending):
  - **(1) the agent's inbound receiver gate.** `validate_channel_message_event` applied the participant-id pattern to `sender_id` and **refused the directive at the transport boundary** (`sender_id is not a valid participant id`) *before* the forced-turn admit ran — `ConveneChannel` returned `202`, the receiver `503`'d delivery, the channel stayed empty. The receiver now carves the two reserved sentinels out of `sender_id` validation, **exactly as it already carves `@everyone` out of mention validation** — a bounded, enumerated set that loosens nothing else about the cleartext-port sender-spoofing trust boundary (PR #248), with a Go↔Python drift guard pinning the carve-out against `ConveneDispatchSenderID` / `SynthesisDispatchSenderID`.
  - **(2) the convener's reply publish.** With delivery unblocked, the convener authored its opener but the mock/plain-text reply path (`synthesize_channel_reply`) auto-@-mentioned the inbound sender — i.e. the synthetic `orchestrator:convene` — so the **opener publish `400`'d** at the orchestrator's publish-path participant-id validation and the discussion never started. A convene/synthesis forced turn is a directed *control* directive, not a peer message, so its reply now addresses the **open floor** (no auto-mention), matching the RFC §B "the opener names no one"; ordinary peer replies keep their reply-to mention.

  **Verified live** on `make demo-autonomous` (mock provider, zero keys, `$0`, zero human turns): convene → the convener opens on the monorepo topic → the chair delivers a real, on-topic **synthesized recommendation** as the closing artifact. ([RFC 0052](docs/rfcs/0052-autonomous-agent-channels.md))
- **RFC 0053 — Gemini live-smoke follow-ups: the Gemini 3.x multi-turn tool loop no longer 400s, and the retired 2.5 demo models are repointed.** Running `MT-PROVIDER-GEMINI-001` live (the RFC 0053 PR 1 smoke) surfaced two breakages the mocked-SDK unit suite could not, both fixed on the PR 2 branch. **(1) `thought_signature` round-trip.** Gemini 3.x emits a `thought_signature` on each function-call part and **requires it echoed back verbatim** on that part in subsequent turns (a `400 … missing a thought_signature` otherwise), so a persona that called a tool over a Gemini 3.x model could not complete the next turn of the loop. The shared [`ToolCall`](agents/llm_types.py) gains an opaque `signature: bytes | None` (default `None`, so `AnthropicProvider` / `OpenAIProvider` / `WatsonxProvider` and every existing `ToolCall` call site are byte-for-byte unchanged); [`GeminiProvider`](agents/llm_gemini.py) captures it off the tool-call part in `_normalize` and replays it on the same part in `append_tool_round`, with `_map_usage` also now folding thoughts tokens for any thinking model (not only 2.5). **(2) Retired 2.5 demo models.** Google retired `gemini-2.5-pro` / `gemini-2.5-flash` for new API users (`generateContent → 404 … no longer available to new users`, even though `models.list()` still advertises them), so the demo alias config, docs, and the `make demo-gemini` banner are repointed to `gemini-3.5-flash` for all three roles (`quality` keeps thinking on; `fast` / `summarizer` keep `thinking_budget: 0`); the §F pricing-drift guard and cost table follow the one physical model. ([RFC 0053](docs/rfcs/0053-gemini-watsonx-providers.md); [MT-PROVIDER-GEMINI-001](docs/manual-tests/MT-PROVIDER-GEMINI-001.md))
- **RFC 0052 — an autonomous discussion no longer stalls forever after its first productive round ([ISSUE-0110](docs/issues/ISSUE-0110-autonomous-productive-round-continuation-stall.md)).** The first live-provider run (claude-sonnet-4-6, the `roundtable` demo) surfaced a wedge none of the shipped surfaces could: a floor round in which the roster **actually replied** ended the discussion permanently — in-round replies are floor-speaker-suppressed by the RFC 0030 Layer 2.5 deferred-fanout contract (on a *human* channel the human is the continuation), the anti-collapse tail reacts only to **silent** rounds, and idle rotation is evaluated lazily on a next publish that never comes — so the interaction sat open with no round 2, no bounded close, and no synthesis. Unreachable before: the offline mock roster is always silent (the mock can't produce valid salience bids → every round took the stall/advance path — the "same opener over and over" texture of `make demo-autonomous`), and the Go acceptance suite's 1ms floor timeout published its replies manually *between* rounds. The fix adds the missing wake, scoped to `autonomous.enabled` (human channels byte-for-byte, regression-pinned): the fanout tail of a productive round **re-fans the round's last reply** as the next open-floor stimulus ([`autonomous_continuation.go`](internal/channels/autonomous_continuation.go)) — the RFC 0052 §Risk "A's post wakes B" cycle — with every shipped bound composing unchanged (`max_rounds` + the wallet soft budget advance at each continued tail; the Layer-0 cascade-depth cap is honoured, never bypassed: an **at-cap** chain is a terminal bound no one can continue past, so it takes the §D structural close — chair synthesis and all — on both the floor and concurrent paths instead of wedging). Companion fix: the publish-path cascade-cap branch now notifies the floor reply waiter before suppressing (the documented Notify-then-suppress posture), so an at-cap in-round reply advances the round instead of burning the full per-speaker turn timeout as `floor_turn{timeout}`.
- **The v0.3.11 release-prep live sweep hardened the §D "always produce an artifact" contract with two fixes found only under real providers ([#763](https://github.com/mkhomutov/Persatrix/pull/763)).** **(1)** Agent wallet RPCs now carry `x-agent-id` gRPC metadata ([ISSUE-0111](docs/issues/ISSUE-0111-anonymous-wallet-rpcs-share-rate-limit-bucket.md)) — they were anonymous to the RFC 0009 per-agent rate limiter, so the bounded-close summary fan (N personas leasing at once) shared one 60 s bucket and starved late personas into the `[interaction summary unavailable]` placeholder. **(2)** An armed channel now **re-fans a duplicate in-window end-vote** instead of bare-suppressing it ([ISSUE-0112](docs/issues/ISSUE-0112-autonomous-duplicate-end-vote-fanout-suppression-wedge.md)): a sub-quorum voting tail (routine under the K/W recency window) whose final duplicate was fanout-suppressed left no way for any bound to fire — no round ever minted again — wedging the unattended discussion open forever (live: 40+ minutes). The re-fanned chain stays a quorum no-op and terminates under the shipped §D bounds; human channels keep the suppression byte-for-byte.
- **The web console topbar no longer doubles the `v` prefix on the version chip** ([#761](https://github.com/mkhomutov/Persatrix/pull/761)).
- **Deterministic memory recall and eviction under order ties — the `rowid` / `trust_score` tiebreak sweep ([#739](https://github.com/mkhomutov/Persatrix/pull/739)–[#747](https://github.com/mkhomutov/Persatrix/pull/747)).** Every memory-store recall path that ordered by a timestamp or FTS rank alone returned tied rows in SQLite-implementation-defined order — recall could differ across engine versions and hosts, and with a `LIMIT` a tie at the cutoff changes *which* rows surface, not just their order. One site was a latent correctness bug outright: under a `created_at` tie, the shared-pool FIFO cap's eviction kept the **oldest** rows instead of the newest ([#746](https://github.com/mkhomutov/Persatrix/pull/746)). Surfaced by the RFC 0044 golden-trace work (a recorded golden's assembled prompt must be byte-portable from the record host to CI, and the eval driver's `FrozenClock` makes same-instant writes routine) but fixed where it belongs, in the production stores: insertion-order `rowid` tiebreaks on fact recall ([#739](https://github.com/mkhomutov/Persatrix/pull/739)), recent-interactions ([#742](https://github.com/mkhomutov/Persatrix/pull/742)), procedural recall ([#743](https://github.com/mkhomutov/Persatrix/pull/743)), closed-interaction recall ([#744](https://github.com/mkhomutov/Persatrix/pull/744)), notes recency/LIKE recall ([#745](https://github.com/mkhomutov/Persatrix/pull/745)), the FIFO eviction cap ([#746](https://github.com/mkhomutov/Persatrix/pull/746)), and the notes FTS5 rank path ([#747](https://github.com/mkhomutov/Persatrix/pull/747)), plus a value-based `trust_score` tiebreak on relationship recall ([#741](https://github.com/mkhomutov/Persatrix/pull/741)). Distinct-timestamp ordering is unchanged, so the recall contracts and every consumer are unaffected (tracking: [#740](https://github.com/mkhomutov/Persatrix/issues/740)).

### 🧪 Testing

- *(RFC 0044 Phase 1 — golden-trace eval harness; a fold-in with no user-facing story, riding v0.3.11 the way RFC 0045 rode v0.3.10)* **`make eval-replay`: recorded golden-trace evals that drive the real persona runtime offline, `$0`, on every PR.** The eval-set recipe format + closed assertion engine ([`schemas/eval_set.schema.json`](schemas/eval_set.schema.json), [`evaluators/`](evaluators/)) with load-time no-vacuous-pass guards ([#735](https://github.com/mkhomutov/Persatrix/pull/735)); the replay LLM client — a `{request-hash: response}` cassette keyed by a volatile-stripping canonicalized SHA-256, fail-loud on a miss so a drifted recipe can never silently pass, with a `RecordingProvider` that records under the **same** hash so a recording is guaranteed replayable ([#736](https://github.com/mkhomutov/Persatrix/pull/736)); the runner + `make eval-replay` / `eval-record` / `eval-drift` targets + the [author guide](docs/evaluators-guide.md), driving each recipe through the **real persona runtime** under a `FrozenClock` `elapsed` temporal seam, with record→replay symmetry proven through the full prompt-assembly path ([#737](https://github.com/mkhomutov/Persatrix/pull/737)); and the first two seed goldens, both replaying green in CI today — [`EVAL-MEMORY-001`](evaluators/eval_sets/EVAL-MEMORY-001.yaml), the [dementia test](docs/manual-tests/MT-MEMORY-005-dementia-test.md) as a five-interaction recall recipe, with the driver forced onto an isolated `:memory:` DB for golden portability ([#738](https://github.com/mkhomutov/Persatrix/pull/738)), and [`EVAL-WORKING-001`](evaluators/eval_sets/EVAL-WORKING-001.yaml), RFC 0034 within-interaction working memory made load-bearing by a new in-process conversation-window fetcher wired when a recipe declares `setup.channel` (channel-less recipes stay byte-identical) ([#748](https://github.com/mkhomutov/Persatrix/pull/748)). The event-asserting seeds (PR 4b) are gated on RFC 0041 typed events → v0.4.0. ([RFC 0044](docs/rfcs/0044-eval-set-golden-traces.md); [PR plan](docs/rfcs/0044-pr-plan.md))
- **The v0.3.11 surface executed live end to end** — the [MT execution report](docs/manual-tests/v0.3.11-execution-report.md) ([#763](https://github.com/mkhomutov/Persatrix/pull/763)): `MT-AUTONOMOUS-001/002/003` full passes on a real provider (both §D close causes observed live), the Gemini/watsonx smokes vendor-billed, the offline `make demo-autonomous` smoke at $0, and the four-vendor headline **Accepted-with-known-gap** — four clouds' models billed in one interaction under one shared cap; the cross-vendor *governed-bidding* leg is tracked as [ISSUE-0113](docs/issues/ISSUE-0113-four-vendor-shared-role-lanes-single-client-mismatch.md) (the shared `fast`/`summarizer` role lanes ride each persona's single provider client). The sweep also made the no-target `make eval-replay` suite sweep runnable (recipe discovery no longer loads the responses fixture as a recipe; [#763](https://github.com/mkhomutov/Persatrix/pull/763)) and captured the [ISSUE-0109](docs/issues/ISSUE-0109-rfc0052-autonomous-defaults-calibration.md) calibration data across seven live arcs.

### 🏗️ Build

- **The `doc_links` gate now validates `#anchor` fragments.** A dangling `file.md#does-not-exist` (or in-page `#anchor`) used to pass the docs link check as long as the *file* existed; the gate now resolves every fragment against the target document's real headings (GitHub slugger semantics, duplicate `-1`/`-2` disambiguation, explicit `<a id>`/`<a name>` anchors), and the enabling sweep fixed 31 stale anchors already in the tree. ([#751](https://github.com/mkhomutov/Persatrix/pull/751))
- **The rfcs-index freshness check now runs locally at pre-commit.** The `docs/rfcs/INDEX.md`-must-match-RFC-front-matter gate was CI-only, so a stale index (e.g. after an RFC status bump) passed the local hook and only failed after push; `scripts/rfcs.py --check` now runs in pre-commit and the failure message points at `make rfcs`. ([#750](https://github.com/mkhomutov/Persatrix/pull/750))

### ⬆️ Upgrade Notes

| Notable change | Detail |
|----------------|--------|
| **[New opt-in capability]** autonomous channels | Arming requires `autonomous.enabled` + a **positive `interaction_budget_tokens` cap** + an `escalation_chair_id` distinct from the convener, **group-only**; a standing schedule additionally requires an aggregate bound (`max_convenings` and/or `standing_budget_tokens`) — all rejected at `validate` otherwise. **Existing channels are unchanged**: every autonomous mechanism (anti-collapse cadence, bounded close, continuation, the vote re-fan) is `autonomous.enabled`-scoped, with human-channel byte-for-byte regressions pinned. |
| **[Upgrade ordering — autonomous adopters]** upgrade orchestrator **and** agents together | The convene/synthesis forced turns carry synthetic `orchestrator:*` sender ids that **pre-v0.3.11 agents reject** at inbound validation — arming a channel needs both sides at v0.3.11. Ordinary (non-autonomous) channels tolerate version skew in either direction. |
| **[Wire — additive, forward/backward compatible]** four `ChannelMessageEvent` fields | `convene = 27` (bool), `close_notification_redelivery = 28` (bool), `close_notification_close_trigger = 29` (string), `synthesis_turn = 30` (bool). A pre-v0.3.11 party leaves/ignores them and keeps the prior behaviour. |
| **[Scope limit]** aggregate bounds are per-process | The standing convening count and spend total are in-memory: an orchestrator restart resets them (no new store migration by design), so a standing channel that had stopped at its bound resumes after a restart. A durable count is a tracked follow-up. |
| **[Providers]** Gemini + watsonx.ai are optional extras | `pip install 'persatrix-agents[providers]'` (or `[gemini]` / `[watsonx]`) pulls the two new SDKs; a base install carries no new runtime dependency. watsonx **fails closed at startup** without a `project_id`/`space_id` (config, not secret); a missing API key stays the lazy S-09 warn. |
| **No store migrates this release** | The channel store stays at **v10** and the persona-memory schema is untouched (the determinism sweep changed result *ordering*, not schema). No upgrade-on-open step. |

## [0.3.10] - 2026-06-27

> **Codename:** Conversations worth posting into

### Highlights

- **Personas now think before they speak — semantic silence ships on by default once a channel is governed.** On an open-floor turn a persona privately decides *whether* the turn is worth a post; if it would only be agreeing, restating, or piling on, it stays silent *with a reason* instead of posting. This generalizes the RFC 0030 Tier-B salience bid into a structured `{ should_post }` verdict on the same leased `fast`-model seam — the idle path stays free, and the private trace never becomes a message, never reaches the channel store, and is never visible to another persona (audit-only). The plan-threaded *considered* compose (`mode: plan`) is a one-rung-up, per-channel operator opt-in. ([RFC 0051](docs/rfcs/0051-reasoning-before-posting.md))

### Upgrade Notes

| Notable change | Detail |
|----------------|--------|
| **[Behaviour — default flip for governed channels]** `reasoning.mode` default `off → bid` | A channel with a salience-gated (`participant` / `chair`) member **defaults to `reasoning.mode: bid`** out of the box — semantic silence on open-floor turns (the structured `{ should_post }` verdict supersedes the numeric score; a turn with nothing to add ends in silence *with a reason*). An **ungoverned** channel is unchanged (the knob is inert there; a non-`off` mode is rejected at `validate`). The flip only fills an **absent** mode — an explicit `reasoning.mode: off` in config is preserved. See [RFC 0051 §G](docs/rfcs/0051-reasoning-before-posting.md). |
| **[Kill switch — one flip]** `reasoning.mode: off` restores the prior score gate | Setting a channel to `reasoning.mode: off` restores **byte-for-byte** the prior RFC 0030 scalar score-gate behaviour at runtime (no restart) and is preserved across a boot. The deliberation egress goes flat (`deliberation.*` emits nothing; the scalar `below_threshold` gate runs instead). The one-flip kill switch is the escape hatch for any deployment that wants the old numeric gate back. |
| **[Behaviour — supersession under bid/plan]** Per-member `threshold` superseded | Under `mode: bid` / `plan`, the `{ should_post }` verdict **is** the silence decision — the per-member numeric salience `threshold` no longer governs the speak/skip call. A deployment that tuned `threshold` floors gets **semantic silence** instead; pin `reasoning.mode: off` to keep the numeric gate. |
| **[Capability-gated — rejected at validate]** `reasoning.depth: deep` not yet deployed | `reasoning.depth: deep` (native extended thinking) needs a provider-protocol change and is telemetry-gated on [RFC 0051 OQ 1](docs/rfcs/0051-reasoning-before-posting.md#open-questions); it is **rejected at `validate`** as an unbacked enum value (a 400, no silent downgrade) until RFC 0051 Phase 4 builds it. `shallow` is the only backed depth this release. |
| **[Opt-in — gated to `mode: plan`]** `reasoning.revise: 0\|1\|2` reflexion loop | The critic→revise reflexion loop is **off by default** (`revise: 0`). `revise ≥ 1` is accepted **only under `mode: plan`** (the critic re-reads the draft against the plan) and **capped at 2** — a `revise ≥ 1` on any other rung, or `> 2`, is a 400. The loop is fail-soft: a hiccup keeps the last good draft and never blocks the post. |
| **[Wire — additive, forward/backward compatible]** `reasoning_mode` / `reasoning_revise` proto fields | `ChannelMessageEvent` gains two additive fields — `reasoning_mode = 25` (string) and `reasoning_revise = 26` (int32) — carrying the channel's resolved rung on the orchestrator → agent dispatch. New field numbers → a pre-v0.3.10 producer leaves them empty/0, which the agent maps to `off` / single-pass, so both are additive across a mixed-version deployment. |

**No store migrates this release.** Unlike v0.3.9 (two channel-store migrations), v0.3.10 advances **no store** — the channel store stays at **v10** and the persona-memory store is untouched. There is **no migration release gate** and no upgrade-on-open step; an already-v10 channel store is a no-op open.

**Version-skew / upgrade-ordering caution.** The orchestrator owns the resolved-rung stamp and the governed-channel default flip. **Upgrade the orchestrator together with (or before) the agents** to arm the flip and the reflexion opt-in; the reverse skew is safe (the fields are additive and default to the prior behaviour — a pre-v0.3.10 agent talking to a v0.3.10 orchestrator simply ignores the new fields).

### 🚀 Features

- **RFC 0051 go-live (PR 6): the resolved `reasoning.mode` rung reaches the agent over the wire, and the governed-channel default flips `off → bid` in lockstep with the kill switch and telemetry.** The router stamps the channel's resolved rung onto every dispatch (`ChannelMessageEvent.reasoning_mode`); the salience seam reads it to pick the bid's verdict grammar (scalar gate / structured silence verdict / plan-threaded compose). Governed channels resolve to `bid` by default at load, boot, runtime-create, and runtime-edit; an explicit `off` is preserved as a kill switch across all of them. New deliberation telemetry — `deliberation.total` (rate), `deliberation.suppressed` (silence charted by `reason_code` + `mode`), `deliberation.duration` (latency histogram), and `deliberation.budget_starved` — makes the active default safe to run. The reasoning cluster — the structured silence verdict + the seam threading + `agent.deliberated` audit, the plan-threaded compose + no-leak test, the `reasoning` config backend, the CLI + web config surfaces, and the `off → bid` go-live flip + telemetry — landed across [#692](https://github.com/mkhomutov/Persatrix/pull/692)–[#697](https://github.com/mkhomutov/Persatrix/pull/697). See the [RFC 0051 PR plan](docs/rfcs/0051-pr-plan.md).
- **RFC 0051 reflexion (PR 8, Phase 5a): an opt-in critic→revise pass that sharpens a `mode: plan` post before it ships, default off.** Under `reasoning.revise: 1|2` a cheap `fast` **critic** re-reads the composed draft against the private plan and, only if it flags weakness, a `quality` **revise** rewrites it — bounded to the round count and **fail-soft** (a parse/critic failure or an exhausted lease keeps the last good draft; the post is never blocked). The critic adds no compose unless it flags weakness, so an `N`-round post costs up to `N+1` quality composes, hard-capped at 2 and metered against the same interaction lease. The discarded drafts and critic notes are walled exactly like the plan (never a message, never persisted) — the privacy wall over both reflexion intermediates is pinned by the no-leak integration test (PR 9). The resolved count rides the wire (`ChannelMessageEvent.reasoning_revise`); `validate` gates `revise ≥ 1` to `mode: plan`. Default `revise: 0` keeps single-pass the norm. Operator telemetry charts the opt-in loop: `reflexion.runs` (by `outcome=revised|noop|degraded` — the draft-changed signal, with a fail-soft hiccup kept separable from a strong-draft no-op so a silent critic/budget outage stays alertable) and `reflexion.rounds` (the revise-round counter). ([#698](https://github.com/mkhomutov/Persatrix/pull/698); the no-leak wall + revise telemetry + closeout [#699](https://github.com/mkhomutov/Persatrix/pull/699))

### ⚡ Performance

- **RFC 0034 Phase 3: the persona conversation-window fetch cache is now bounded, and the window is instrumented.** The in-process cache that fronts the per-turn transcript fetch was unbounded — over a long-lived orchestrator serving many channels it grew without limit. It is now a bounded LRU (process-global capacity, `agents/persona_runtime/_conversation_window_cache.py`). New OTEL metrics make the window tunable from data: `conversation_window.cache_access` (`result=hit|miss`), `conversation_window.cache_evictions`, `conversation_window.fetch_duration`, and `conversation_window.fallback` (`reason=fetch_failed|fetch_none` — the silent degrade-to-current-event-only that could otherwise mask a history-endpoint outage). The `max_turns` / `max_tokens` defaults are unchanged; their data-driven retune is the one remaining follow-up, gated on collecting this telemetry. ([RFC 0034](docs/rfcs/0034-persona-conversational-working-memory.md); [#700](https://github.com/mkhomutov/Persatrix/pull/700))

### 📚 Documentation

- *(operator/persona guides + README/ROADMAP)* The [persona-agents guide](docs/guides/persona-agents.md) §*Reasoning before posting* (the `reasoning.{mode,model,depth,revise}` knob — `bid` = semantic silence on by default once a channel is governed, `plan` = the considered plan-threaded compose, `off` = the one-flip kill switch; the silence reason observable at `reason_code` granularity; the `depth: deep` deferral + the `revise` gating to `mode: plan`) and the [memory-architecture diagram](docs/diagrams/memory-architecture.md) note that the walled reasoning trace is **deliberately outside** the memory diagram were authored during implementation ([#699](https://github.com/mkhomutov/Persatrix/pull/699)) and verified against the shipped config surface (the RFC 0050 `reasoning` knob, `persatrix channel config`, web `ChannelSettings`) and the resolved-rung router; the README Roadmap row + the ROADMAP Version Map / RFC Master Index (RFC 0051 + RFC 0034 → `✅ Implemented`) were refreshed and the [v0.3.10 release checklist](docs/v0.3.10-release-checklist.md) landed ([#705](https://github.com/mkhomutov/Persatrix/pull/705)).
- *(v0.3.10 planning + release-prep)* The v0.3.10 [master plan](docs/v0.3.10-plan.md) + the [RFC 0051 PR plan](docs/rfcs/0051-pr-plan.md) ([#691](https://github.com/mkhomutov/Persatrix/pull/691)), the [release-prep plan](docs/v0.3.10-release-prep-plan.md) ([#702](https://github.com/mkhomutov/Persatrix/pull/702)), and the [MT execution report](docs/manual-tests/v0.3.10-execution-report.md) ([#703](https://github.com/mkhomutov/Persatrix/pull/703); the `agent.deliberated` audit `extra=` egress fix [#704](https://github.com/mkhomutov/Persatrix/pull/704)).

### 🧪 Testing

- The headline manual test [`MT-REASON-001`](docs/manual-tests/MT-REASON-001.md) *reasoning before posting — semantic silence with a reason, a plan-threaded considered post, and the walled private trace* was executed **live on Anthropic** against the `main` RC tip: an **80% semantic suppress-rate** under `bid` (`deliberation.suppressed{reason_code=nothing_to_add}` = 8 / `deliberation.total` = 10), plan-threaded considered posts with the private plan markers **absent** from the transcript **and** from both peers' `?as_participant` recall, a reflexion `revised`×2 with the discarded draft walled, and the one-flip `off` kill switch zeroing the deliberation egress and surviving a boot; `reasoning.depth: deep` / `revise ≥ 1` outside `mode: plan` (and `> 2`) rejected at `validate`. See the [execution report](docs/manual-tests/v0.3.10-execution-report.md).
- Structural release-blocker gates green on the RC tip (live on host): the **privacy-wall no-leak integration test** ([`tests/integration/test_deliberation_no_leak.py`](tests/integration/test_deliberation_no_leak.py) — pins zero `messages` rows + zero appearance in a second persona's reconstruction for **both** the private `CompositionPlan` *and* a discarded reflexion draft + critic note, the load-bearing correctness gate), the `off → bid` go-live + kill-switch + capability-gate + reconcile-survives-boot + proto-stamp Go suites ([`config_reasoning_test.go`](internal/channels/config_reasoning_test.go) / [`channel_config_reasoning_test.go`](internal/server/channel_config_reasoning_test.go) / [`fanout_salience_test.go`](internal/channels/fanout_salience_test.go) / [`grpc_dispatcher_salience_test.go`](internal/channels/grpc_dispatcher_salience_test.go)), the Python deliberation / plan / reflexion + idle-cost telemetry suites, and the RFC 0034 P3 cache/instrument suite. The carried-forward recall / governance / convergence + alias cost-attribution + session/epoch isolation gates stay green, and the **cost-regression** gate confirms reasoning is net-neutral-to-saving — it reuses the existing leased `fast`-model seam, **saves** the compose on a suppressed turn (no new wallet origin), and the idle-cost invariant holds.

### 🏗️ Build

- *(RFC 0045 — open-core policy + dependency-direction CI gate; a fold-in with no user-facing story)* The `MIT ← BUSL ← Private` dependency-direction CI gate — `make imports-check` (a Python `import-linter` contract) + the Go `internal/archpolicy` check — plus the [reserved-seams note](docs/open-core-reserved-seams.md) and the DCO scaffold. Pure license-boundary infrastructure: it **moves no code**, stands up no private track, and adds no shipped-artifact dependency (the `import-linter` gate is dev/CI-only). The per-extraction RFCs (0046/0047) stay v0.4.0+. ([#701](https://github.com/mkhomutov/Persatrix/pull/701))
